The two test vectors are orthogonal with squared norms ell and ell eta.
Adding d_pre in (C24) and projecting onto them proves, for every minimizer,

    ||Z_0-z_star||^2
      >=ell[(B_U+a_U)_+^2+(B_V+a_V)_+^2/eta].       (C25)

Here u_+=max(u,0). Negative projection lower bounds correctly contribute
zero. Taking the infimum over the entire minimizer set preserves (C25).
The model keeps all other components; they can only add squared distance.

Whenever its denominator is positive, (C22) and (C25) give

    V_actual
      <[1/1500+(101/100)N_3]
        /[(B_U+a_U)_+^2+(B_V+a_V)_+^2/eta].         (C26)

This retains the actual two action components and intrinsic terminal norm.
It is a necessary estimate, not a replacement for the full state.

## II.6. Performance obstruction for changing transverse-neutral prefixes

Under (C4), a_V=0. Since z>1 and e<3/5, B_V>999/1000.
Since eta<21/50,

    B_V^2/eta>(999/1000)^2(50/21)>7/3.

Therefore (C23),(C25) imply the uniform strict obstruction

    V_actual<(121/30000)/(7/3)=121/70000<1/500.      (C27)

This includes arbitrary changes and sign changes in scalar prefixes aligned
with E U, as well as multivariate prefixes whose full net displacement,
including the bridge, is orthogonal to E V. All rich values, scale, affine
placement, common optimum, ambient Gram, and final gradient remain free.
It does NOT assert that arbitrary scalar orientations satisfy (C4).
At ell=0 the final point is a queried minimizer and the objective gap is0.

On the SAME physical word, direct expansion gives

    S_parent+1=(4951+3501sqrt(2))/50<200,

using sqrt(2)<10/7. The accepted scalar Huber witness of factb98bb807d514e80d
has H_global(H)=1/(2S_parent+1)>1/400. That fact gives K_63(H)>=H_global(H),
and fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives F_ray_63(H)>=K_63(H). Hence

    V_actual/H_global(H)<121/175<1,
    V_actual/K_63(H)<121/175,
    V_actual/F_ray_63(H)<121/175.                  (C28)

The common Huber member already dominates the whole stated model; no
maximization of the stronger accepted scalar witnesses is needed.
The changing example (C5) obeys this bound.

Its original benchmark ratio is exactly B_63 V_mix with V_mix from (C12).
Internal capacity and capacity squared have not replaced B_63.
This is a performance obstruction, not infeasibility or an upper bound
on unrestricted W_63(H). It does not decide the original arbitrary-h goal.

## II.7. Necessary escape condition for an arbitrary prefix

Drop (C4). Suppose a legal mixed trajectory in II.1 attains at least the
same-word accepted Huber payoff. That payoff is >1/400, so (C23),(C25)
necessarily give

    (B_U+a_U)_+^2+(B_V+a_V)_+^2/eta<121/75.         (C29)

Using B_U>17/5, B_V>999/1000, eta<21/50 and the rational inequalities

    121/75<(13/10)^2,
    (21/50)(121/75)=2541/3750<(5/6)^2,

we obtain the two separate strict necessary conditions

    a_U<-21/10, a_V<-497/3000.                    (C30)

If a positive-part argument is nonpositive, the corresponding inequality
follows from the stronger bound a_U<=-B_U or a_V<=-B_V. No sign case is lost.

These are conditions on sum h_j p_j+beta_bridge p_31 on the original word.
Any useful prefix must place its initial point sufficiently behind the
rich start in BOTH first-atom gradient directions. A change confined to
the U direction, regardless of signs, cannot do so.
(C29)--(C30) are not sufficient for interpolation or improved performance,
and they are not a universal additive radius cost or an all-depth law.

## II.8. Exact interface for a changing child

The full finite interface does not require constant prefix gradients.
For all rich jets, including the actual final jet and common optimum,
use coordinates relative to c and set

    y_i=x_i-c-g_i,
    A_i=f_i-<g_i,x_i-c>+||g_i||^2/2,
    Delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

For each prefix jet (Z_j,p_j,v_j), put

    r_j=Z_j-c-p_j,
    B_j=v_j-<p_j,Z_j-c>+||p_j||^2/2.

All values here are above the common minimum. At the optimum sample,
g_star=f_star=A_star=Delta_star=0 and y_star=z_star-c.
Expanding BOTH cross orientations proves their exact equivalence to

    max_i{A_i+<g_i-p_j,r_j>}
       <=B_j<=min_i{Delta_i-<p_j,y_i>}, for each j. (C31)

The rich index i includes every sample and the optimum. Also retain every
rich internal row and every prefix internal row

    B_j-B_k+<p_j-p_k,r_k><=0 for distinct j,k.      (C32)

The heights B_j of a changing prefix are distinct variables in general.
No shared-height or endpoint-only reduction has been made. The physical
identities (C3), full vector/Gram constraints, and the free terminal jet
remain present. The system is simply a rewriting of ALL original rows,
so by accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 it is necessary and sufficient for
finite interpolation. It does not glue independently selected functions.

For a feasible list, translate its chosen optimum to0 and use (C10).
The exact minimizer set of this particular static interpolant is

    C_zero=conv({0} union {x_i-g_i: f_i-||g_i||^2/2=0}),       (C33)

with all prefix, rich and terminal samples included. Every delta_i is
nonnegative by the reverse optimum row. In each fixed direction v,
sufficiently small positive t makes the linear part of H_mix(tv) equal
the support function of this finite hull. A point in the hull has conjugate
value0. For a point outside, the direction from its closest hull point
strictly separates it, and a sufficiently small positive t gives a
strictly positive conjugate value. This proves both inclusions in (C33).

A witness must still use dist(x_initial,C_zero)^2. All gradient dependencies,
values, heights, residuals and distance costs are retained in this system.
The two-action estimate is a consequence, not a substitute.

## II.9. Scope attacks, retrieval and outcome

The following points were checked explicitly.

* The new gradient change is used by the actual first update. All4160 rows
  were covered, including incoming terminal rows.
* The sign-changing example (C12s) has one common static function, an actual
  bridge and a nearest-minimizer radius; the sign pattern was not specified
  independently of the joint interpolation system.
* Equal zero-residual heights at3,27 are exact, but the nonzero residual at7
  costs chi kD+delta r_0N in (C19). It was not removed.
* The terminal square keeps every component of the final gradient.
* The optimum cancels in (C15); no origin alignment was imposed.
* Changing prefixes do not inherit the preceding constant-prefix distance
  monotonicity: f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, step3 gives x_1=-2, doubling the distance.
  This is a counterexample to that general algebraic extension only, not a
  claimed instance of the fixed rich31 model. The new proof instead prices
  actual action in (C25).
* Different epsilon in (C5) leaves all rich jets and the final value fixed
  but changes the true radius. Those rich terminal data alone therefore
  cannot determine a mixed normalized-payoff recursion.

Retrieval followed search-math-results in mature-subproblem, mutation/repair
mode, targeting finite smooth convex interpolation of changing prefix data.
Matlas recovered1910.01845, Theorem5. Web search located the known exact
paper1502.05666, Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods.
The exact local downloads/1502.05666.txt was read at Theorem4 and its proof,
Corollary1, and Remark3. Its finite interpolation definitions and Q orientation
were checked. The constructive quadratic-conjugate mechanism and the need
for longer cycles explain why all cross rows remain. No new external theorem
is imported: the used construction is already proved in fact1252b8823bb5a290.

This supplies changing and sign-changing prefixes and a scoped performance obstruction
beyond constant gradients, plus a necessary escape condition for arbitrary
prefixes. It does not supply an improving adversary, close arbitrary scalar
orientations or heterogeneous children, give an all-depth mixed recursion,
or cover the original arbitrary-h/non-dyadic interface. Every new assertion
remains part of this joint UNVERIFIED draft.

# An actual SAME-H_m cap return outside the screened initialization range

Status: **UNVERIFIED joint counterexample**, within the one selected binary
character-pyramid mechanism. This tests a scope extension, not a second
allocated policy or a claim of favorable terminal performance. No trajectory,
arithmetic evaluation or recurrence was executed; all formulas are textual.

The false extension is: for the full capped binary-character pyramid, the
cap can never reactivate after the first cone query on H_m, even if the
initial transverse coordinates are outside their base intervals. The
base-bounded hypothesis of the main joint screen is essential.

## Full static function, word and real geometry

Use the SAME original H_m, m>=1536, tau=1/4, with

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), e=1/(4m), beta=1-e,
    c=lambda(1+e), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Every child retains these enclosing parameters. The full physical horizon
is n=2^m-1 and the original denominator is B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 with every
original split retained. No performance comparison to B_n is asserted here.

Choose q=1, b_1=3, identity metric on R^2, and the full character orbit of
Z/2Z with its two weights1/2 and real slopes

    v_+=(1,3), v_-=(1,-3), both offsets0,
    D=conv{0,v_+,v_-},
    F(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Put l=1+a, k_bridge=1+beta, T=2l+k_bridge, and start at

    x_0=(T,3(1+T)).

This initial point is fixed from the known full word. Its transverse
coordinate exceeds the base width3, so it is outside the declared screen.
Its carrier is positive and at least1. The function is everywhere finite,
convex differentiable and1-smooth: its gradient is the unique Euclidean
projection on D, whose two normal inequalities imply nonexpansiveness.
Comparing the attained maxima proves differentiability. D has interior,
so its least smoothness is1. This is one static function for the whole run.

For completeness the full word-to-face rule, applicable also after the
counterexample prefix, is as follows. At any x=(u,v), let t in[0,1] minimize
(t-u)^2+(|v|-3t)_+^2. Its derivative test is

    Psi_x(t)=t-u-3(|v|-3t)_+.

If Psi(0)>=0 use t=0; if Psi(1)<=0 use t=1; otherwise use the interior
root, either t=u when |v|<=3u or t=(u+3|v|)/10 on the saturated cell.
The unique actual gradient is (t,clip(v,[-3t,3t])). Each physical update is
x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i), at every i<n including all later original
steps. This exact finite piecewise formula specifies all queries and the
actual terminal, without imposing a desired later trajectory or resetting m.

## Actual first cap exit and reactivation

The first three original steps are l,k_bridge,l. For i=0,1,2 let
A_i be the mass of those steps already spent and C_i=T-A_i. At these
queries

    x_i=(C_i,3(1+C_i)), C_i>=l>1,
    grad F(x_i)=v_+.

For the projection threshold Psi(1)=1-C_i-9C_i=1-10C_i<0. The clipped
transverse coordinate is3. This proves the actual cap gradients and their
literal updates. The values are F(x_i)=10C_i+4. After all three steps,

    x_3=(0,3),
    g_3=(9/10,27/10),
    r_3=x_3-g_3=(-9/10,3/10).

The unconstrained cone threshold is (0+3*3)/10=9/10<1. Thus x_3 really
is a query with strictly inactive cap, gradient on the positive lateral
edge, normal r_3 and F(x_3)=81/20. The normal satisfies
r_(3,0)+3|r_(3,1)|=0 and <r_3,g_3>=0, which directly proves this projection.

The NEXT ORIGINAL step at index3 is the level2 bridge

    H=1+beta*c>3.

Indeed beta*c=lambda(1-e^2)>2: use lambda>12/5 and e<=1/64 to get
(12/5)(4095/4096)>2 by cross multiplication. After this step,

    x_4=(-9H/10,3-27H/10).

The claimed actual new gradient is the OPPOSITE capped vertex v_-.
Its residual is r_4=(-9H/10-1,6-27H/10). The full vertex normal tests are

    <r_4,v_->=(36/5)H-19>0,
    <r_4,v_+-v_->=6(6-27H/10)<0,
    <r_4,0-v_->=-[(36/5)H-19]<0.

The first positivity and both strict negativities follow from H>3.
Every convex combination of the three vertices passes the same normal
test, so P_D(x_4)=v_- and the cap has ACTUALLY reactivated. Its value is
F(x_4)=(36/5)H-14. No freely switched matrix or assigned gradient is used.

## Every original row, complete minima and TRUE initial distance

For ANY two samples of this one function, put g_x=P_D(x), r_x=x-g_x and
f_x=<x,g_x>-||g_x||^2/2. The direct algebraic identity is

    f_x-f_y+<g_x,y-x>+||g_x-g_y||^2/2
        =<r_y,g_x-g_y><=0.

The last inequality is the projection normal comparison on the full D.
This proves both orientations of every original finite, remote, terminal
and minimizer row, including all later samples from the full word rule.
The entire Gram is a Gram of the displayed real vectors. It is PSD without
a finite-field metric or expected-covariance assertion.

The complete old minimum set and value are

    min F=0,
    argmin F={(u,v):u+3|v|<=0}.

If u+3|v|>0, a small positive multiple of a corresponding vertex gives
positive objective; otherwise every objective term is nonpositive and zero
attains the maximum. The closest minimum to this x_0 is NOT zero. It is

    x_closest=(-9/10,3/10),
    x_0-x_closest=(T+9/10,3(T+9/10)),
    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=10(T+9/10)^2.

The closest point lies in the displayed polar. Its difference from x_0
lies on the positive cone ray; the two vectors are orthogonal. For any
other polar point y, the positive-ray normal gives
<x_0-x_closest,y-x_closest><=0, proving nearest-point optimality and its
uniqueness. This pays the actual initialization rather than replacing its
radius by1 or by ||x_0||.

## Precise impact

The complete word, full function, actual future query map, every original
row, full minimum and true initial distance are specified. The cap return
at query4 refutes ONLY removal of the initial base-coordinate hypothesis
from the cap-capture conclusion. It does not refute the proved-in-draft
base-bounded screen, and does not assert a useful terminal payoff for this
outside-base run. A local value increase cannot be silently promoted into
an all-depth or original-profile lower bound.

The remaining genuine initialization freedom is now explicit: an initial
transverse vector outside the orbit's base intervals can preserve enough
normal state to reactivate a finite cap at an original long bridge. Whether
such trajectories in higher rank can supply a useful full-word/profile
lower witness remains OPEN. No additional policy, round or formal call is
allocated by this counterexample.

Glossary: D,F,v_+,v_- are the real two-character dictionary, its quadratic
support conjugate and full slopes; x_i,g_i,r_i,f_i are actual query points,
gradients, full projection normals and values. T,l,k_bridge,H,A_i,C_i are
the specified initialization mass, first leaf, level1 bridge, ACTUAL level2
bridge and prior mass/remaining carrier on the first three steps. Other
hierarchy notation is defined in the first display. x_closest is the actual
closest minimum. All new mathematics is UNVERIFIED, with no fact_id.

# A two-dimensional obstruction at a saturated primitive cut

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or verifier
call. The only existing fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for its explicit finite
smooth-convex interpolation construction. Every other identity is proved here.

## 1. Definitions and an energy identity

For a nonnegative physical schedule h of length n let T_tot=1+sum_i h_i.
For normalized smoothness 1 and an optimum jet (0,0,0), put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
          -T_tot(T_tot-1)||g_n||^2.

Here primitive validity means V_h>=0 on every legal convex 1-smooth GD
trajectory. Suppose the sampled data additionally satisfy

    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 for every i,
    x_n=g_n.

The first condition is exactly Q_(i,star)=0; the second gives
f_n=||g_n||^2/2. Expanding

    ||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2
       =2 sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2

therefore gives the exact identity

    V_h=||x_0||^2-T_tot^2||g_n||^2.                 (1)

For any such data, whether or not yet jointly interpolable, write y_i=x_i-g_i.
Direct substitution also gives

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                             (2)

## 2. General two-bridge construction

Let a,b,c be arbitrary finite nonnegative blocks, with capacities

    A=1+sum a_i, B=1+sum b_i, C=1+sum c_i.

Take r_L,r_R>0 and h=(a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c). Define

    D_tail=B+C+r_R, T_tot=A+D_tail+r_L,
    eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,
    p_aux=-B/r_L.

The first scalar array is a legal Huber trajectory: start at A and use
derivative clip(x,-eta_cut,1). During a and at its endpoint the gradient is
1. Its endpoint is 1. The first bridge lands at -r_L=-eta_cut D_tail.
Every remaining query has gradient -eta_cut, and the final point is
-eta_cut. All the data satisfy the hypotheses of (1), so

    V_base=A^2-T_tot^2 eta_cut^2.                   (3)

The second scalar array is defined as follows. During a use constant
gradient p_aux, start at A p_aux, and end at p_aux. The first bridge lands
at -r_L p_aux=B. On the remaining schedule (b,1+r_R,c) use the ACTUAL
Huber trajectory with derivative clip(x,-beta_inner,1), starting at B.
The b block ends at 1, the second bridge lands at -r_R=-C beta_inner,
and c ends at -beta_inner. Assign every value by
f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. Thus (1) yields

    V_aux=A^2 B^2/r_L^2-T_tot^2 beta_inner^2.        (4)

The second array is generally NOT interpolable across the first bridge.
It is only auxiliary data; its possible violations are accounted for below.

For epsilon_mix>0, combine both arrays into two-dimensional data

    X_i=(x_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) x_i^aux),
    G_i=(g_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) g_i^aux),
    F_i=f_i^base+epsilon_mix f_i^aux.

The GD equations hold coordinate by coordinate, all Q_(i,star) remain zero,
and X_n=G_n. Consequently

    V_mix=V_base+epsilon_mix V_aux.                 (5)

Here is a complete interpolation audit. Partition the samples into L (a
and its endpoint), M (b and its endpoint after the first bridge), and R (c
and its final endpoint after the second bridge). Overlaps are assigned to
the group whose gradient is used at the relevant bridge. The groups are
disjoint in original time: L includes the first bridge query, M includes
the second bridge query, and R includes the terminal sample.

* Within L all gradients in each scalar array agree, so Q=0.
* Within M union R the auxiliary data are one legal Huber trajectory;
  the base gradients agree, so all Q<=0 there.
* From L to M, the auxiliary target has y_j>=0 and the source gradient
  p_aux<1, so Q_aux<=0. From M to L, the auxiliary target y_i is a
  nonnegative multiple of p_aux<0 and 1-p_aux>0, so Q_aux<=0.
* From L to R, if R_j is the remaining sum of physical c steps after its
  target sample j, the two residuals are

      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)eta_cut R_j,
      Q_aux=beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)R_j.

* From R to L, if H_i is the remaining sum of physical a steps after its
  target sample i, then

      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)H_i,
      Q_aux=(-beta_inner-p_aux)p_aux H_i.

Zero remaining sums give zero in both arrays. Thus duplicate positions
caused by zero steps do not require a separate limiting argument.

The optimum-outgoing inequalities hold too: Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,Y_i>,
and the inner product is nonnegative separately in each scalar array.
For the auxiliary L data this is H_i p_aux^2>=0, and in the other groups
it follows directly from their plateau coordinates. Q_(i,star)=0 already.

When r_L r_R<BC, one has -p_aux>beta_inner. The ONLY potentially positive
auxiliary residuals are L-to-R, and all interpolation inequalities hold for

    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,
    epsilon_max=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)
                    /[beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].             (6)

When r_L r_R>BC the ONLY potentially positive auxiliary residuals are
R-to-L, and all inequalities hold for

    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,
    epsilon_max=(1+eta_cut)
                    /[(-p_aux)(beta_inner+p_aux)].                 (7)

When r_L r_R=BC both cross families are nonpositive for any epsilon_mix.

All these are complete original-index interpolation checks, including both
cross directions and both optimum directions.

## 3. One fixed globally legal function

Include the zero optimum sample and define, on R^2,

    H_mix(v)=max_i { c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },
    c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i=||G_i||^2/2,
    f_mix(x)=sup_v { <x,v>-H_mix(v) }.

The maximum includes i=star. This is the explicit finite interpolation
construction of accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290, proof section 2, applied to
the entire finite data set. The checked Q inequalities imply that f_mix
interpolates every (X_i,G_i,F_i), is convex differentiable and 1-smooth,
and has f_mix(0)=0=min f_mix. Thus the mixed data are one actual GD
trajectory of one fixed function; they are not an adaptive oracle or a
reset of the function at a bridge.

## 4. A universal quantitative two-cut necessary condition

Any primitive h as above necessarily satisfies

    r_L r_R [A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] <= A^2 BC.           (8)

First assume r_L r_R<BC and choose epsilon_mix=epsilon_max in (6).
Using D_tail=B+C+r_R, substitution into (3)-(5) gives

    V_mix = (BC+r_R D_tail)(D_tail+r_L)
              /[D_tail^2 r_R(BC-r_L r_R)]
             * {A^2 BC-r_L r_R[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)]}.

The prefactor is positive, proving necessity of (8) in this case. For an
algebra check, before the final factorization the numerator is

    (BC+r_R D_tail)
       {A^2[BC(D_tail+r_L)-r_L r_R D_tail]
                                     -T_tot^2 r_L^2 r_R}.

Use A^2 D_tail+T_tot^2 r_L
=(D_tail+r_L)[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] to obtain the displayed factorization.

If r_L r_R>BC, set z_aux=-p_aux=B/r_L and beta_inner>z_aux, and use (7).
Equation (5) becomes

    V_mix=(beta_inner+eta_cut z_aux)/(beta_inner-z_aux)
             * {A^2-T_tot^2[(1+eta_cut)beta_inner/z_aux-eta_cut]}.

The bracket is strictly smaller than A^2-T_tot^2<0. Hence no such schedule
is primitive. If r_L r_R=BC, V_aux=z_aux^2(A^2-T_tot^2)<0, so sufficiently
large epsilon_mix in the fully legal mixed data again gives V_mix<0.
This excludes the two remaining cases and completes (8).

No quadratic-balance assumption about a,b,c appears in this argument.

## 5. Saturated first cut forces its inner tail cut

Suppose in addition r_L T_tot=A D_tail. Then eta_cut=A/T_tot, V_base=0,
and A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)=A T_tot. Condition (8) reduces to

    T_tot r_L r_R<=ABC,
    equivalently r_R D_tail<=BC.                   (9)

Therefore a positive defect r_R D_tail>BC in the inner tail cannot be
repaired by any signed storage or full collection of interpolation edges
while keeping this outer cut exactly saturated. This statement concerns
primitive validity of this actual schedule, not all objective certificates.

The geometry also explains the conclusion without (8): at outer saturation
(4) becomes (T_tot/D_tail)^2[B^2-D_tail^2 beta_inner^2], which is strictly
negative exactly when the tail cut fails. Any sufficiently small positive
mixture allowed above then has V_mix<0.

## 6. Explicit refutation candidate for the outstanding eight-step seed

Set u=10/7, C=27/7, A=B=4, and

    D_tail=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,
    r_R=D_tail-55/7,
    T_tot=(4+D_tail+sqrt((4+D_tail)^2+16D_tail))/2,
    r_L=T_tot-4-D_tail.

The schedule is

    (3/2,3/2,1+r_L,3/2,3/2,1+r_R,10/7,10/7).

Directly from these root equations,

    r_R D_tail=196/9, r_L T_tot=4D_tail,
    BC=108/7<196/9.

Thus (9) fails. Here is a concrete mixing scale without an unspecified
smallness parameter. Define eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,
p_aux=-4/r_L, and

    epsilon_mix=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)
                      /[2 beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].          (10)

It is positive: the first defining polynomial has its positive root in
(10,101/10), and substituting 5/2 into
r_L^2+(4+D_tail)r_L-4D_tail shows r_L<5/2. Hence
r_L r_R<(5/2)(157/70)=157/28<108/7=BC. Formula (10) is half of (6).

For reference the two position arrays are

    base: (4,5/2,1,-r_L,-r_L+(3/2)eta_cut,
           -r_L+3eta_cut,-C eta_cut,-(1+u)eta_cut,-eta_cut),
    aux:  (4p_aux,(5/2)p_aux,p_aux,4,5/2,1,
           -r_R,-(1+u)beta_inner,-beta_inner).

The gradient arrays are

    base: (1,1,1,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,
                           -eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut),
    aux:  (p_aux,p_aux,p_aux,1,1,1,
                           -beta_inner,-beta_inner,-beta_inner).

Values are g_i x_i-g_i^2/2 in each coordinate. Sections 2-3 show that
their mixed two-dimensional jets come from the explicit fixed f_mix.
Finally beta_inner D_tail=1372/243=4(343/243), so

    V_mix = epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2
                  [16-(1372/243)^2]
          = -epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2 * 937600/59049 < 0.        (11)

This gives an exact concrete primitive counterexample for the proposed
eight-step seed. It closes that seed's proposed primitive amplification.
It makes no claim that a different direct objective certificate is
impossible, and does not settle the unrestricted asymptotic target.

## Literature and verification status

Targeted Matlas and web retrieval supplied no theorem used in this proof.
The primitive/Huber regime had already been audited in previous rounds;
this continuation uses the accepted finite interpolation construction
rather than an external black box. All new results above remain candidates
pending a selected verifier allocation. No fact was submitted or written.

# max66: a complete-prefix objective multiplier law and its exact scoped failure

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No formal submission,
no new fact, and no unrestricted objective upper bound are asserted.
The original exact-profile minimax problem remains OPEN.

Outcome of this draft: on the unchanged tau=1/4 words, the symmetric
kernel fails the full PSD test for every m>=3. Its sole backward-tilt
repair, including every nonnegative parameter choice in the displayed
family, fails at the Huber level for every m>=4. In fact any PSD level
within this same law would have to exceed 1/100, so this law cannot even
provide a vanishing objective bound on that sequence. These are scoped,
unverified statements about a proposed certificate family, not W itself.

One mechanism is tested: a product kernel on every directed queried-prefix
pair, completed by explicit minimizer and terminal multipliers. The base
kernel is symmetric. The ONLY repair increases every backward multiplier
by a second product kernel. Both the concrete positive rule and the whole
nonnegative two-parameter family are addressed. This is a whole-word
objective calculation, not a primitive-capacity assumption or a modular
boundary inequality.

## 1. Exact object, accepted inputs, and notation

For a fixed enclosing integer m>=1, put

 lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
 c=lambda_s(1+1/(4m)), a=beta_word/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

Every child in these definitions uses this SAME enclosing m. In particular,
the left word H_(m-1) is not the standalone word with enclosing m-1.
The letter n always denotes the number of actual physical updates. The
terminal gradient is an interpolation variable and supplies no extra update.

The accepted mathematical inputs are:

* fact1252b8823bb5a290: original smooth-convex interpolation, the full
  formal-vector objective certificate convention, and the existence of a
  selected certificate without incoming terminal rows. Its complete proof
  was read. No queried-prefix row deletion follows from that theorem.
* fact04219554e41d9750: the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark recurrence and its
  stated denominator enclosures. Only its unconditional benchmark is used
  in Section 9. No conditional seed substitution or new composition is used.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The independent formal vectors are

                       x_0,g_0,...,g_n.

Define x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t, and use the accepted convention

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, i!=j.

All original directed pairs, including the minimizer and terminal, remain
part of the model. A zero multiplier is an explicit choice for this candidate,
not a claim that its row is generally redundant. The certificate polynomial is

 M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j) lambda_ij Q_ij.       (1)

If every multiplier is nonnegative, all value coefficients cancel, and
M_cert is PSD on this entire space, then W_n(h)<=q_cert. This is the
W=2R normalization with the original specified-minimizer distance. The
contrapositive about a PARTICULAR polynomial is not a lower bound on W.

To avoid collisions with earlier project notation, the new objects below
have suffix66. For an arbitrary nonnegative word of length n, let

 S66=sum_(i<n)h_i, D66=1+2S66,
 p66_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=i<=n,
 T66=sum_(i<n)h_i*p66_i,
 d66_i=sum_(j<i)h_j*(p66_i-p66_j), 0<=i<n.                 (2)

T66=(S66^2-sum h_i^2)/2. Indeed, both sides sum h_i*h_j over j<i.
Thus T66>0 exactly when at least two steps are positive. Sums below are
finite mathematical formulas; none was evaluated by a program.

## 2. One explicit complete-prefix kernel, with its sole repair

Suppose first that S66>0. For real u66,v66>=0, put, for distinct i,j<n,

 K66_ij=h_i*h_j*(u66+v66*1_(i>j)), K66_ii=0,
 r66_i=sum_(j<n)K66_ij-sum_(j<n)K66_ji
       =v66*h_i*(2p66_i+h_i-S66),
 mu66_i=h_i*[2+u66*(S66*p66_i-T66)+v66*d66_i]
              /(2S66-p66_i),
 ell66_i=mu66_i+r66_i, 0<=i<n,
 mu66_n=2-sum_(i<n)mu66_i.                               (3)

The denominator 2S66-p66_i is at least S66>0, including at zero steps.
Specify EVERY original multiplier as follows:

 lambda_ij=K66_ij for distinct i,j<n;
 lambda_(i,star)=mu66_i for 0<=i<=n;
 lambda_(n,i)=ell66_i for 0<=i<n;
 lambda_(star,i)=0 for 0<=i<=n;
 lambda_(i,n)=0 for 0<=i<n;
 lambda_ii=0.                                           (4)

The base is v66=0. The sole repair is v66>0, which retains every forward
prefix pair and adds mass on every backward prefix pair. These are dense
arrays, not thresholded supports extracted from numerical data. The
choice of no incoming terminal rows is compatible with accepted1252, but
the product form (3) is a NEW RESTRICTION of this candidate only.

Here is a completely explicit, strictly sign-valid choice, so (3) is not
just a feasible-set restatement. If T66>0, let b66 be the smallest positive
step and set

 u66=1/T66,
 mu66_i^0=h_i*(1+S66*p66_i/T66)/(2S66-p66_i),
 mu66_n^0=2-sum_(i<n)mu66_i^0,
 J66=sum_(i<n)h_i*d66_i/(2S66-p66_i),
 v66=min{1/[4S66(S66-b66)], mu66_n^0/(2J66)}.             (5)

The base candidate uses the same u66 and v66=0. Formula (5), without
numerical fitting or optimization, is the one repaired candidate.

All divisions and signs in (5) are valid. For h_i>0, p66_i<S66 and
mu66_i^0>0. Directly from (2),

 sum_(i<n)(2S66-p66_i)*mu66_i^0
    =sum_(i<n)h_i*(1+S66*p66_i/T66)=2S66.                (6)

Because every positive term has 2S66-p66_i>S66, (6) gives
sum mu66_i^0<2, so mu66_n^0>0. At the second positive step d66_i>0,
which gives J66>0. Two positive steps also give S66-b66>0. Consequently
v66>0. Moreover

 mu66_i=mu66_i^0+v66*h_i*d66_i/(2S66-p66_i)>=0,
 mu66_n=mu66_n^0-v66*J66>=mu66_n^0/2>0.                 (7)

For a positive h_i, the definition of mu66_i^0 gives
mu66_i/h_i>=1/(2S66). Since h_i>=b66 and p66_i>=0,

 ell66_i/h_i>=1/(2S66)-v66*(S66-b66)>=1/(4S66)>0.       (8)

At h_i=0, mu66_i=ell66_i=0 and every K66 edge incident to i is zero.
All K66 edges between positive steps are strictly positive in BOTH
directions. Thus every coefficient of (4), including the terminal and
minimizer coefficients, has its stated exact sign. The unrepaired v66=0
choice is also nonnegative, with ell66_i=mu66_i^0 and mu66_n=mu66_n^0.

## 3. Complete flow cancellation and the exact Huber-null equations

For i<n, the full outgoing-minus-incoming flow of (4) is

 mu66_i+sum_j K66_ij-sum_j K66_ji-ell66_i=0.             (9)

Every finite prefix edge contributes once to each of its row/column sums,
so sum r66_i=0. At n the outgoing flow is

 mu66_n+sum_(i<n)ell66_i=2,                             (10)

and no edge enters n. At star, the outgoing flow is zero and the incoming
flow is sum_(i<=n)mu66_i=2. Therefore (1) has zero coefficient on every
formal f_i, including f_n. This is the full original value identity.

The finite-prefix drift of the proposed kernel is exactly

 sum_(j<n)K66_ij*(p66_i-p66_j)
  =u66*h_i*(S66*p66_i-T66)+v66*h_i*d66_i.                (11)

Equations (3) and (11) give

 (2S66-p66_i)*mu66_i
      =2h_i+sum_(j<n)K66_ij*(p66_i-p66_j), i<n.         (12)

We also check the terminal equation rather than assuming it. Multiply
r66_i by p66_i and sum to obtain

 sum_i p66_i*r66_i=sum_(i,j<n)K66_ij*(p66_i-p66_j).

Summing (12) consequently yields

 sum_i(2S66-p66_i)*mu66_i=2S66+sum_i p66_i*r66_i.

Using sum r66_i=0 and ell66_i=mu66_i+r66_i then gives

 sum_(i<n)ell66_i*(S66-p66_i)=S66*mu66_n.               (13)

Together (12)--(13) are all the finite-node Huber-null balance equations.
They were derived here; the unverified finding0fda is provenance, not a
premise. The equations by themselves DO NOT imply PSD.

## 4. Full original matrix, including all cross-block entries

Let C66 be the symmetric (n+1)-gradient coefficient matrix of (1). The
complete full matrix is

              M66(q_cert)=[[q_cert,-mu66^T/2],
                           [-mu66/2,C66]],             (14)

where mu66=(mu66_0,...,mu66_n). This is a matrix on the original n+2
coordinates; it is not a matrix restricted to realizable trajectories.

For clarity we derive its entries from ALL original rows. A term
lambda_ij*||g_i-g_j||^2/2 contributes half the total incident mass to
the diagonal and minus lambda_ij to the corresponding cross coefficient.
Only a forward edge out of i contributes -h_i*lambda_ij to ||g_i||^2
through <g_i,x_j-x_i>. By (9)--(10) the half incident mass is
out_i-1_(i=n). Thus, with h_n=0,

 C66_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(j>i)lambda_ij.            (15)

For 0<=i<j<=n, the difference-square terms contribute
-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji). A term based at i with destination after j
contributes -h_j*lambda_ik. A term based at j with destination at or
before i, or at star, contributes h_i times its multiplier. Therefore

 2C66_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)
          -h_j*sum_(k>j)lambda_ik
          +h_i*(mu66_j+sum_(k<=i)lambda_jk).             (16)

All sums in (15)--(16) except the explicitly separated star term are
over finite sample indices. Substituting (3)--(4) gives every entry:

 C66_ii=mu66_i+h_i*[u66*(S66-h_i)+v66*p66_i]
                 -u66*h_i^2*(S66-p66_i-h_i), i<n;
 C66_nn=1;                                            (17)

 2C66_ij=h_i*mu66_j+h_i*h_j*[-(2u66+v66)
                 -u66*(S66-p66_j-h_j)
                 +(u66+v66)*(p66_i+h_i)], i<j<n;       (18)

 2C66_(i,n)=-ell66_i
             +h_i*(mu66_n+sum_(t<=i)ell66_t), i<n.     (19)

These expressions include every left/right cross-block coefficient and
every terminal gradient coordinate. No product of child remainders or
independent-gradient assumption has replaced them. The candidate is formed
on the whole parent word; no child slack was assumed zero or discarded.

Here is also a coordinate-free expansion checking the same whole matrix.
Write z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t, so x_i=x_0+z_i and z_star=0. The value-free
polynomial is exactly

 q_cert||x_0||^2-sum_(i<=n)mu66_i*<g_i,x_0>
 -sum_(i<=n)mu66_i*<g_i,z_i>+sum_(i<=n)mu66_i*||g_i||^2/2
 +sum_(i<n)ell66_i*[<g_n,z_i-z_n>+||g_n-g_i||^2/2]
 +sum_(i!=j<n)K66_ij*[<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].   (20)

In particular, both the entries and their linking to the full matrix are
exact polynomial identities; no numerical matrix is substituted.

Let e66 be the all-ones vector of length n+1. Multiplying the gradient
part of (20) by e66 gives the row identity

 2C66*e66=2h+mu66+drift66,
 drift66_i=p66_i*mu66_i
                +sum_(j finite)lambda_ij*(p66_i-p66_j). (21)

For a direct verification, the squared differences contribute mu66/2
to C66*e66. The bilinear position terms contribute half their row drift
and half the vector sum of their displacements. The latter sum is
-2z_n=2sum h_t*g_t, because full finite flow is 2 at n and all star
outgoing multipliers vanish. This gives (21) on every coordinate.
Equations (12)--(13) now imply

              C66*e66=D66*mu66/2.                     (22)

Consequently M66(1/D66)*(1,e66/D66)=0 exactly. More generally, the
Schur completion in the initial-point coordinate is

 M_cert=q_cert*||x_0-sum_i mu66_i*g_i/(2q_cert)||^2
      +sum_(i,j)[C66_ij-mu66_i*mu66_j/(4q_cert)]<g_i,g_j>,
                                                        (23)

when q_cert>0. At q_cert=1/D66 this Schur remainder annihilates e66,
but will have a strictly negative diagonal in Section 7.

The accepted terminal-square parameterization is respected as well:
alpha_star=mu66_n/2, alpha_i=ell66_i/2. Equations (9)--(10) show that
these sum to one, and their prefix flow is 2alpha_i. Hence the FULL
identity from fact1252 applies with exactly these coefficients, without
any deleted prefix direction. Nothing here assumes its remainder PSD.

## 5. Exact geometry of the unchanged hierarchy

For m>=3, let

 k66=2^(m-1)-1, r66= h_(k66)=1+a*c^(m-1),
 A66=sum H_(m-1), Jleft66=sum_(i<k66)h_i^2,
 E66=A66*r66-Jleft66, Kroot66=3A66+2r66.                (24)

r66 is the physical root step, not r66_i of (3). The whole word is
palindromic, with left/right sums A66, so

 S66=2A66+r66, p66_(k66)=A66,
 T66=2A66^2+2A66*r66-Jleft66,
 d66_(k66)=(A66^2+Jleft66)/2,
 S66*A66-T66=-E66,
 T66-E66=A66*S66.                                     (25)

The identity for d66_(k66) follows by writing
sum_(i<k66)h_i*p66_i=(A66^2-Jleft66)/2 within the actual left block.
Every child step is strictly smaller than r66, because c>1 and it has a
strictly smaller level. Thus 0<Jleft66<A66*r66 and E66>0.

We will need A66>=r66. This is proved inside each enclosing m. Let
s_j=sum H_j and t_j=1+a*c^j. Then

 s_(j+1)=2s_j+t_j, t_(j+1)=c*t_j-(c-1).

For m>=3, 2<c<3 and 0<beta_word<1. Since

 s_2=3+2a+beta_word, t_2=1+beta_word*c,
 s_2-t_2=2+beta_word*(1+2/c-c)>0,                       (26)

the base s_2>t_2 holds. To see the sign without a decimal, c<3 gives
1+2/c-c>-2; multiplication by beta_word in (0,1) keeps the product
strictly above -2, whether that parenthesis is positive or negative.
If s_j>=t_j, then s_(j+1)>=3t_j>c*t_j-(c-1)=t_(j+1).
Induction through j=m-1 gives A66=s_(m-1)>t_(m-1)=r66.

Here c<3 follows from c<=13lambda_s/12 and lambda_s<5/2; the latter is
sqrt(2)<3/2. Also beta_word>=11/12 and lambda_s>12/5, so r66>3 for
m>=3. For m>=4 the simpler estimate

 r66=1+beta_word*c^(m-2)
       >=1+(3/4)*lambda_s^(m-2)>4                     (27)

holds. These are symbolic inductions/inequalities, not executed recurrences.
The first step h_0=1+a is a smallest positive step and is at most A66.

## 6. First-query nonnegativity constrains the entire repaired family

We now allow ANY u66,v66>=0 in (3), not just the explicit choice (5).
Suppose all of (4) are nonnegative. Only one necessary sign, ell66_0>=0,
already imposes a strong condition. Put b=h_0>0 for this section. Since
p66_0=d66_0=0,

 ell66_0=b*[(2-u66*T66)/(2S66)-v66*(S66-b)]>=0.

Therefore

 u66*T66+2S66*v66*(S66-b)<=2,
 u66<=2/T66, v66<=1/[S66(S66-b)].                      (28)

If (28) is violated, an original terminal multiplier is negative, which
already disqualifies that parameter choice. The remaining argument grants
every other sign and still excludes PSD in the stated range.

At the root, (3), (17), and (25) give exact scalar coefficients

 mu66_(k66)=r66*[2-u66*E66+v66*d66_(k66)]/Kroot66,
 C66_(k66,k66)=mu66_(k66)
                   +r66*A66*[u66*(2-r66)+v66].        (29)

Let mu_r denote mu66_(k66) only in the rest of this proof. Because E66>0,
d66_(k66)>0, and (28) holds, (25) gives

 mu_r>=2r66*(1-E66/T66)/Kroot66
      =2r66*A66*S66/(Kroot66*T66)
      >=r66*S66/[Kroot66*(A66+r66)]>0.                (30)

The last inequality uses T66<=2A66*(A66+r66), since Jleft66>=0.

For r66>2, the u66 term in the root diagonal of (29) is nonpositive.
Using (28), (30), and b<=A66 gives

 [v66*r66*A66]/mu_r
 <= A66*Kroot66*(A66+r66)/[S66^2*(S66-b)]
 <= A66*Kroot66/S66^2
 < 3/4.                                               (31)

The last strict sign is exactly

 3S66^2-4A66*Kroot66=4A66*r66+3r66^2>0.

Thus the COMPLETE original root gradient diagonal satisfies

                 C66_(k66,k66)<(7/4)*mu_r.             (32)

On the other hand, A66>=r66 implies

 Kroot66*(A66+r66)<=(10/9)*S66^2,                       (33)

because 10S66^2-9Kroot66*(A66+r66)
=(A66-r66)*(13A66+8r66)>=0. From D66>2S66, (30), and (33),

                     D66*mu_r>9r66/5.                (34)

No step in (28)--(34) assumes PSD or a certificate on an isolated child.
These are necessary constraints for the explicitly defined WHOLE-WORD
kernel with exact nonnegative original multipliers.

## 7. A negative direction in the entire original space

Let q_cert=chi66/D66 with chi66>0. On the two ORIGINAL formal coordinates
(x_0,g_(k66)), the restriction of (14) is precisely

        [[q_cert,-mu_r/2],[-mu_r/2,C66_(k66,k66)]].     (35)

To link this to the full space, take an arbitrary unit Euclidean vector e
and assign

 g_(k66)=e, g_i=0 for every other i including i=n,
 x_0=mu_r*e/(2q_cert).                                (36)

This is an explicit linear embedding of the two-coordinate space into the
original n+2 coordinates. All positions still use their original GD
substitution. Evaluating (20), or completing the square in (35), gives

 M_cert=C66_(k66,k66)-mu_r^2/(4q_cert)
       < (mu_r/4)*[7-9r66/(5chi66)].                  (37)

Hence the full matrix is NOT PSD whenever chi66<=9r66/35. In particular,
for EVERY enclosing m>=4, (27) gives 9r66/35>36/35>1, so the exact Huber
level chi66=1 fails. Its explicit negative value obeys

                    M_cert<-mu_r/20<0.                (38)

This failure applies to the strictly positive rule (5), the symmetric
base, and every other nonnegative u66,v66 completion in this family.
If some other coefficient fails its sign first, that choice is already
excluded before the PSD test. The conclusion is not based on a numerical
eigenvalue or a removed block.

For the base v66=0, (29) gives the stronger bound C66_(k66,k66)<=mu_r.
Equations (34)--(36) therefore exclude the exact Huber level already at
EVERY m>=3, since 9r66/5>27/5>4. The repaired-family assertion above
starts at m=4; no assertion of its PSD feasibility or failure at m=2,3
is required or inferred.

Assignments (36) are formal quadratic-form tests. They are NOT claimed
to be interpolation-feasible transcripts or actual convex functions.
Their role is legitimate because a full dual remainder must be PSD on
ALL original formal vectors. They do not give a lower witness for W.

Any PSD member at a positive level must instead have

                     chi66>9r66/35.                  (39)

If q_cert<=0, the nonzero off-diagonal -mu_r/2 in (35) makes PSD
impossible too. Thus no nonpositive-level exception is being omitted.
Since r66 tends to infinity by (27), (39) excludes every bounded
multiplicative loss relative to 1/D66 within this same multiplier law.
Changing u66,v66 with m or with the chosen level does not evade (28)--(39).
Changing the formulas for mu66 or terminal weights is outside this law.

## 8. Zero physical steps and the small degenerate cases

The target words themselves have every h_i>1. No physical bridge vanishes
at any claimed m. Nevertheless the coefficient definitions have a complete
zero-step interpretation, which is useful for detecting hidden coordinate
identifications.

If S66>0 and T66>0, formulas (2)--(5) apply even with zero steps. Every
zero-step index i<n has h_i=mu66_i=ell66_i=0 and no incident K66 edge.
Equations (17)--(19) make its entire gradient row and column zero.
For nonzero indices, the same formulas depend only on their actual
chronological prefix masses. Thus, after selecting the original indices
of positive steps and the ORIGINAL terminal n, the full matrix is exactly
T_pick^T*Mhat66*T_pick, where T_pick is the coordinate selection map and
Mhat66 uses the compressed positive word. This is a full-space congruence
with zero omitted columns. No equality between formal duplicate gradients
is imposed. This is also the explicit lifting convention in accepted1252.

If S66=0, including n=0, set only lambda_(n,star)=2 and q_cert=1.
Then M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2 on the entire original space. Every unused
gradient coordinate is a zero column.

If S66>0 but T66=0, exactly one physical step is positive, say h_i=b.
Use u66=v66=0 in (3). The only nonzero multipliers are
lambda_(i,star)=lambda_(n,star)=lambda_(n,i)=1. With D66=2b+1,
the full completion is

 M_cert=(1/D66)||x_0-(D66/2)(g_i+g_n)||^2
                     +(3-2b)||g_i-g_n||^2/4.          (40)

All other original coordinates are free zero columns. Formula (40) is
PSD at the Huber level exactly when 0<b<=3/2; for b>3/2 its difference
direction is negative. This finite calibration is not the claimed
unbounded bridge. For m=1 the target b=1+3/(5lambda_s)<3/2, so the
one-step calibration succeeds. The formula (5) is never divided by zero
in these degenerate cases.

## 9. A stronger limitation on this law, and benchmark orientation

The necessary loss in (39) is not merely a small excess above one.
For m>=4, a=(4m-1)/[(4m+1)lambda_s]>=15/(17lambda_s)>1/3,
because lambda_s<5/2 and 6/17>1/3. For j=m-1, counting the actual
occurrences of each hierarchical step gives

 A66=2^j-1+a*(c^j-2^j)/(c-2).                          (41)

This identity also follows by substitution into s_(j+1)=2s_j+1+a*c^j,
with s_0=0; it is not an evaluated recurrence. Since c>lambda_s>2,
1/(c-2)<=1/(lambda_s-2)=lambda_s<5/2 and 2^j<=c^j. Hence

 A66< c^j+a*c^j/(c-2)
       =a*c^j*[1/a+1/(c-2)]<(11/2)*r66.

It follows that S66<12r66 and, because r66>4,

                       D66<97r66/4.                  (42)

Combining (39) with (42), any member of this law that is actually PSD
at any level necessarily has

 q_cert=chi66/D66>9r66/(35D66)>36/3395>1/100.           (43)

Equation (43) is a LOWER RESTRICTION ON CERTIFICATE LEVELS IN THIS FAMILY.
It is NOT a lower bound on W_n(h), and does not assert that a feasible
member at such a larger level exists.

For completeness, the original benchmark in fact042 is
B_0=C_0=1 and B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l), where
G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. Its split k=0 gives
B_n>=G(1,B_(n-1))>=B_(n-1)+2. Induction yields B_n>=2n+1.
Therefore a feasible upper level from this law would obey

 q_cert/U_n^ZJ=B_n*q_cert>(2n+1)/100 -> infinity
                                      for n=2^m-1.    (44)

In particular it cannot establish a fixed relative improvement below
the original U_n^ZJ at unbounded true horizons. No new benchmark DP or
asymptotic numerical value has been used. There is no actual full-class
upper estimate to combine with the newly accepted stagewise-family
comparison, and the NEGATIVE alternative of PROBLEM.md is not proved.

## 10. Source/data provenance and precise limits

Assignment: TASK.md, completed baseline65, one round through ceiling66,
attempted ceiling67. Initial guidance404a833640d56440, later guidance
b038406fdc2969cd explicitly retaining this assignment. Plan finding
3fe1a6b901887c36. The task allows one analytic repair and no formal gate.

The complete previous discovery ad24079f80cd6a3a was read from
research/small_tau_full_pep_20261001/final_handoff.md. Its existing public
raw files H3/raw.json, H4/raw.json, H5/raw.json, H6/raw.json were opened.
The complete H3 original primal Gram, values, pair labels/multipliers and
dual matrix were read; larger cases were inspected through original first,
terminal, and root rows, saved values/diagnostics and directed multiplier
rows. Those are data reads, not regenerated calculations. Some initial
broad output was truncated; decisive root/sign/model passages were then
reread in bounded output. No claim of inspecting every entry of the larger
arrays is made.

The observed stored root multipliers are markedly directional and vary
among earlier queries. This motivated one backward-tilt repair of a dense
kernel. It did NOT justify equal ratios across backward pairs, or any
exact zero, rank, positivity or PSD assertion. Equations (3)--(5) are
explicit analytic candidate restrictions and are not represented as an
exact reconstruction of the raw arrays. The stored original primal and
dual spectra have small negative eigenvalues. The old six-job allocation
is CLOSED; no old diagnostic was recomputed and no numerical evidence is
a premise of Sections 2--9.

The complete full-certificate finding0fda962c46583d52 and the historical
terminal-reselection draft792aa4b6dbbad8bf were read. Only the latter's
accepted extension1252 is a correctness input. The old four-fan finding
2105aae5913d003a was recalled for its exact restricted scope; the present
law is not a new name for a child endpoint-fan gluing. The boundary result
2f7305e3a99f9978 was read for its contracts and scope: it excludes a
particular modular recharge inequality and expressly leaves whole-parent
certificates open. Neither result is an assumed impossibility premise.

Primary-source retrieval used the search-math-results skill with stage
active_program, mode repair, intent proof-method background, and missing
mechanism full original multiplier signs plus all-depth PSD. Matlas
returned no directly useful theorem for the query. Web retrieval located
Jason M. Altschuler and Pablo A. Parrilo, "Acceleration by Stepsize
Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization,"
arXiv:2309.16530 (2023), Sections 2--3, Theorem 3.4 and Appendix A.2:
https://arxiv.org/pdf/2309.16530 . The exact PDF was downloaded. Its
definitions, proof structure, helper lemmas and deferred quadratic-form
proof were read through the web PDF text and saved as two local text
extracts. The local pdftotext command was unavailable (exit127), and the
immediately following read of the missing text file returned exit2. The
web HTML v4 request had returned404. No package was installed.

The source uses Q^AP_ij=-2Q_ji in our convention and proves a recursive
certificate for h_t=1+lambda_s^(valuation_2(t+1)-1). Its exact Pell mass
and bridge identities differ from our enclosing-m-dependent word. The
method suggests retaining complete interpolation terms before checking
small matrix restrictions; its silver-word theorem is not imported as an
upper bound for this different schedule. No external theorem or computer
algebra script is used as a premise of the obstruction. In particular no
Matlas result is asserted applicable and no external_refs submission occurs.

The result is confined to formulas (3)--(4), u66,v66>=0, with their
Huber-null minimizer/terminal completion. It does NOT exclude pair- or
level-dependent kernels, different completions at higher objective levels,
other terminal selections, hierarchical corrections, general full duals,
or the existence of a Huber-tight upper certificate for these words.
It supplies no non-Huber lower witness, and no lower bound on unrestricted
W or on the minimax value. It does not invoke the stagewise construction
as an upper estimate. The original question remains OPEN.

## 11. Resource closeout

# One shared array for all chronological three-block tests

Status: self-contained candidate, **not verifier accepted**. This continuation
uses no optimization, new launch, installation, or fact_submit. The accepted
dependencies are `1252b8823bb5a290`, for smooth-convex interpolation and the
original finite full-objective formulation, and `0e7e78056dc6258c` only for
the explicit orthogonal-subclass limit in Section8. Every new statement below is
proved within this document. In particular an earlier unverified cut identity
or recursive identity is not a correctness premise.

The requested decision at q=1/M_m is NOT obtained: no feasible common array,
and no infeasibility proof for the entire collection, is asserted. What is
obtained is an exact attained legal-primal interpretation of the collection,
its common two-jump matrix, a quantitative optimum-source budget, and a
quantitative rejection of one explicitly constructed scalable common array
family by two cuts already in the collection.

## 1. Exact collection and notation

Let n>=2, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) have nonnegative finite entries, and put
S=sum h_i, M=1+S, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, including P_n=S. Normalize L=1 and
x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The original independent formal vectors are
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n); x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct sample indices
in I={star,0,...,n}, write

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Choose ONE finite nonnegative array lambda_ij with finite-node flow

 sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n).                 (1)

Its value-free quadratic polynomial is

 C_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.        (2)

For every 1<=k<l<=n, require (2) to be nonnegative whenever

 g_i=u for i<k, g_i=v for k<=i<l, g_i=w for i>=l,         (3)

with x_0,u,v,w independent arbitrary formal vectors. The same array is used
for every (k,l). This is the collection A_2. One-block and two-block tests
are included by equality of block values. All the earlier queried-j/tail
tests are included: for j>0 use k=j,l=j+1,u=0; for j=0 use a two-block
specialization. No position moment, optimum edge, or initial coordinate is
discarded in (2).

## 2. One common matrix, with all singular cases

Introduce chronological jumps d_k=g_k-g_(k-1), 1<=k<=n, and b=g_0. Then
g_i=b+sum_(k<=i)d_k. This is an invertible linear coordinate change. Write
the matrix of (2) in coordinates (x_0,b,d_1,...,d_n) as

              [ C       R ]
 A_lambda =   [ R^T     T ],                              (4)

where C is 2 by 2, r_k is column k of R, and T is n by n. A restriction
(3) is EXACTLY the principal block of (4) on x_0,b,d_k,d_l. Thus the entire
collection is equivalent to

 C>=0;
 r_k in Range(C) for every k;
 Z_kk>=0 and Z_kl^2<=Z_kk Z_ll for every k<l,              (5)
 Z_kl=T_kl-r_k^T C^+ r_l.

Here C^+ inverts positive eigenvalues and vanishes on Ker(C). To prove the
singular statement, diagonalize C. A nonzero component of any r_k in its
kernel makes the corresponding quadratic unbounded below in a free base
variable. Once every such component vanishes, complete the squares in the
positive base coordinates. The remaining two-variable quadratic has matrix
[[Z_kk,Z_kl],[Z_kl,Z_ll]], whose PSD criterion is exactly (5). This proves
both implications, including zero diagonal entries and C=0.

There are n(n-1)/2 coupled pair tests of ONE residual matrix Z. This is not
independent per-partition feasibility. It is also not full PSD of Z.

Here is an explicit matrix-only distinction. In any three jump coordinates
take diagonal entries 1 and all off-diagonal entries -3/4, with every other
jump diagonal 1 and other off-diagonals zero; take C=I_2 and R=0. Every
two-jump principal matrix is positive definite, but the vector with these
three jumps equal to 1 has quadratic value 3-9/2=-3/2. This exhibits the
three-jump correlation absent from A_2. It does NOT assert that this matrix
is realized by a nonnegative interpolation array for a particular h.

## 3. Quantitative budget for actual optimum-source edges

Set mu_i=lambda_(i,star), nu_i=lambda_(star,i), and nu=sum_i nu_i.
Summing (1) gives sum_i mu_i=2+nu. On the constant-gradient subspace
g_i=b, direct expansion gives

 C = [ q                -(2+nu)/2 ]
     [ -(2+nu)/2        1+2S+sum_i(1+P_i)nu_i ].          (6)

For the lower-right coefficient, finite edges contribute
sum_i P_i(out_i^finite-in_i^finite)
=2S-sum_i P_i mu_i+sum_i P_i nu_i. The finite-to-star position terms
add back sum_i P_i mu_i; the optimum-edge norm terms add 1+nu. The x_0
coupling is -(sum_i mu_i)<x_0,b>. These are exactly (6).

At the proposed q=1/M, positivity of C is equivalent to

 sum_i (S-P_i)nu_i + (M/4)nu^2 <= S.                     (7)

Indeed its determinant is
1-1/M-sum_i(S-P_i)nu_i/M-nu^2/4, and its first diagonal is positive.
In particular nu<=2sqrt(S/M), with a stricter penalty on early
optimum-source mass. This uses the actual optimum edges, not a choice to
delete them. The budget alone does not prove infeasibility, since nu=0
remains allowed.

## 4. The legal primal cone represented by the collection

Let V_(k,l) be the subspace of real coordinate lists
(X,G_0,...,G_n) in which G is constant on the three blocks (3), while X is
free. Define the cone

 K_2=sum_(1<=k<l<=n) { positive semidefinite matrices with range in V_(k,l) }.
                                                                    (8)

Equivalently, K_2 consists of finite sums vv^T where each scalar gradient
list in v has at most two chronological jumps. This follows by diagonalizing
each positive semidefinite summand. Conversely each such vv^T lies in one
of the summands, after inserting an unused cut if necessary.

Thus G belongs to K_2 if and only if it has a Gram realization in a finite
Euclidean space with an orthonormal coordinate system in which every
coordinate of (g_0,...,g_n) changes at most twice. Different coordinates may
use different cuts. The x_0 coordinate in each of these directions is free.
This is substantially more flexible than a whole vector gradient with only
two jumps: many cut patterns coexist in a single trajectory.

There is also an exact intrinsic test on the incremental Gram matrix. In
jump coordinates write a candidate Gram matrix as [[A,B],[B^T,D]], with
the first two coordinates x_0,b. It lies in K_2 if and only if it is PSD
and D is a sum of PSD matrices each supported on at most two jump indices.
Necessity follows from (8). For sufficiency choose D=VV^T with every
column of V supported on at most two indices. Full PSD gives
B D^+D=B and A-B D^+B^T>=0 by the square-completion argument in Section2.
The columns of [[B D^+V],[V]] give the prescribed B,D and base block
B D^+B^T. Add base-only columns factoring A-B D^+B^T. Every resulting
column is in an allowed subspace, proving the claim, including singular D.

Equivalently, D admits nonnegative allocations z_ij for ordered distinct
indices with

 sum_(j!=i)z_ij<=D_ii,    z_ij z_ji>=D_ij^2.             (8a)

To prove this description, group every two-index PSD summand by its support
{i,j}; its off-diagonal must total D_ij, and its two diagonal allocations
give the displayed determinant condition. Conversely insert the PSD matrix
[[z_ij,D_ij],[D_ij,z_ji]] on each pair and add the nonnegative leftover
diagonal entries. The resulting sum is exactly D. This does not require
that the two-jump coordinate components be individually interpolable:
the full summed Gram matrix and the common function values obey the
interpolation constraints together.

The cone is closed. If G_r converges and G_r=sum_a G_(r,a) is a decomposition
over the finitely many summands of (8), each summand is PSD and has trace at
most trace(G_r). In fixed finite dimension every PSD matrix has norm bounded
by its trace. Pass to a common subsequence on which all summands converge;
their ranges remain in their closed subspaces and their limits are PSD.
Their sum is the limit of G_r and belongs to K_2.

The cone is full dimensional in the space of symmetric (n+2)-matrices. In
jump coordinates every pair of coordinates occurs in one allowed block
{x_0,b,d_k,d_l}. Therefore the linear map from the direct sum of the
symmetric block spaces onto the ambient symmetric space is surjective.
It has a linear right inverse. Starting from identity matrices on all these
blocks, sufficiently small ambient perturbations can be lifted by this
right inverse while every block remains positive definite. The sum of the
embedded identities is consequently an interior point of K_2.

For n>=3 this is a PROPER subcone of the full PSD cone. Choose v with three
nonzero jumps. If vv^T=sum_a G_a with all G_a PSD, then every vector orthogonal
to v has zero quadratic value in each G_a, so Range(G_a) is contained in
span(v). Every nonzero summand is therefore a positive multiple of vv^T.
Such a summand cannot be supported on an allowed two-jump subspace. Hence
vv^T is not in K_2. This is a cone statement, not a claim that the particular
v is a legal GD transcript.

Finally, by (8), a matrix has nonnegative pairing with every G in K_2 if and
only if every restriction (3) is PSD. Thus (5) is exactly the dual-cone
condition K_2^*, with the original array flows imposed separately.

## 5. Exact attained equality on the actual positive-step family

Assume now h_i>1 for every i<n, as holds for every critical H_m below. Let
w_2(h) be the maximum of 2f_n over G in K_2, G_(x0,x0)<=1, and every original
ordered constraint Q_ij<=0 including the optimum. Then

 w_2(h) = min { q : some common lambda>=0 satisfies (1) and A_2 at q }.
                                                                    (9)

Both extrema are attained. The left side is an actual legal smooth-convex
GD performance over the coordinate class described above, not an aggregate
of independently chosen functions. In particular w_2(h)<=W_n(h).

Here is a proof including strict feasibility rather than an assumed generic
strong-duality theorem.

First construct a strict interpolation point within K_2. Put

 kappa=min(1,min_i(h_i-1))/4 >0,
 r=kappa/[2(kappa+1)], a_i=r^i,
 g_i=sqrt(a_i)e_i, f_i=(kappa+1/2)a_i,
 x_0=(kappa+2)sum_(i=0)^n sqrt(a_i)e_i+e_extra,             (10)

where the displayed coordinate vectors are orthonormal. For i<j, write
delta_i=f_i-a_i/2=kappa a_i. Direct substitution of orthogonality into the
two finite interpolation constraints shows they are strict exactly when

                   a_j < delta_i-delta_j < (h_i-1)a_i.   (11)

For the lower inequality use a_j/a_i<=r and
kappa(1-r)-r=kappa/2>0. For the upper use
delta_i-delta_j<kappa a_i<(h_i-1)a_i. The optimum residuals are
Q_(i,star)=-a_i and Q_(star,i)=-kappa a_i. Thus ALL ordered residuals are
strict. The extra coordinate makes the full Gram matrix positive definite.
Each gradient coordinate in (10) is supported on one query, hence has at
most two jumps; the extra coordinate has zero gradients. The Gram matrix
lies in K_2. Scale all vectors by one small positive number and all values
by its square to make the radius inequality strict as well.

This point may be on the boundary of K_2. Add a sufficiently small positive
multiple of the interior point constructed in Section 4 to its Gram matrix,
holding the values fixed. Finitely many strict linear inequalities remain
strict for small enough perturbations, including the radius. A point of a
convex cone plus an interior point lies in its interior: translate a small
ball in the cone by the cone point. This gives full conic strict feasibility.

The primal feasible set is compact. Every feasible Gram matrix is a genuine
finite set of Euclidean vectors. Adding Q_(i,star) and Q_(star,i) gives
||g_i||^2<=<g_i,x_i>, hence ||g_i||<=||x_i||. Inductively
||x_i||<=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), and all Gram entries are bounded by
Cauchy--Schwarz. Also ||g_i||^2/2<=f_i<=||x_i||^2/2. The cone is closed by
Section 4; all constraints are closed. Compactness proves primal attainment.
The interpolation assertion in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies a legal
finite-dimensional smooth convex function for each feasible full transcript.
The maximum is positive: take x_0=1, every g_i=t=1/(2S+1), and
f_i=t-(P_i+1/2)t^2. Finite-pair and finite-to-star residuals vanish, while
Q_(star,i)=-t+(P_i+1)t^2<0. This constant-gradient Gram matrix is in K_2 and
2f_n=t>0. Homogeneous scaling consequently forces an optimal transcript to
have ||x_0||=1, so the maximum agrees with the normalized objective ratio.

For explicit separation, form the open convex set of tuples (s,t,c) for
which some G in K_2 and real f obey s_ij>Q_ij, t>G_(x0,x0)-1, c<2f_n.
The point (0,0,w_2) is outside. Separate it, obtaining nonnegative
coefficients (lambda,rho,eta) on (s,t,-c). If eta=0, substitution of the
strict feasible point makes the limiting separation inequality strictly
negative, contrary to separation. Hence eta>0; normalize eta=1. Because
the values f_i are unrestricted in the separation inequality, their
coefficients vanish and give exactly (1). Scaling G in K_2 implies that
the matrix of C_lambda with q=rho lies in K_2^*. Setting G=f=0 gives
rho<=w_2. The matrix condition and lambda>=0 give the reverse weak-duality
inequality w_2<=rho. Thus equality holds and the common array is attained.
This is the same elementary separation mechanism as the accepted full-cone
proof, applied here only after proving its needed hypotheses for K_2.

Consequently, on H_m, failure of the entire common-array relaxation at
q=1/M_m would have an actual legal witness in this coordinate class with
2f_n>1/M_m. This conclusion relies on (9), not on regarding any formal
negative vector of a particular certificate as a legal trajectory.

## 6. An explicit scalable common array that fails a retained two-cut test

This section screens one concrete construction; it does NOT disprove common
array feasibility in (9).

For a schedule a of sum A-1, define the intrinsic polynomial

 V_a=2sum_i a_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i a_i(1-a_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -(A-1)A||g_end||^2.

Take two identical step lists of capacity A, separated by a physical bridge
1+r, and put t=r/A, T=(2+t)A. Let p be the bridge gradient, z the final
gradient, and H the weighted sum of gradients at the right-child steps.
Set W=H+z, and use right-child indices j in

 G=sum_j a_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p),
 J=sum_j a_j Q_(j,z),
 k=(2+t)^2, u=2(2+t), v=2A,
 jcoef=2+6t+2t^2, delta=2-(1+t)^2.

The following identity holds for EVERY r>=0, including supercritical r:

 V_join=V_left+k V_right-u G-v Q_(p,z)-jcoef J
                                      +delta||W-Ap||^2.  (12)

For a direct coefficient derivation, put C_r=G+rQ_(p,z)+J. Expanding the
actual positions gives

 C_r,value=2sum a_j f_j+(r-A)f_p+(2-A-r)f_z,
 C_r,gradient=sum a_j(1-a_j)||g_j||^2
                  +[(A-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(A+r)||z||^2/2,
 V_right-2J=||W||^2-A^2||z||^2,
 Q_(p,z)=f_p-f_z-(r+1/2)||p||^2+||z||^2/2-<p,W>.

The cross terms in the second line cancel using
2sum_j a_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)a_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j a_j^2||g_j||^2.
These four displayed expansions verify (12) in all values and gradient
coefficients, by substituting the definitions of k,u,v,jcoef,delta. No
positivity of delta is used in this polynomial identity.

Start with the empty array at a leaf. Recursively retain the left array,
multiply the right array by k, and add uG+vQ_(p,z)+jcoef J. All actual
coefficients are nonnegative for r>=0. If R_left and R_right are the two
value-free intrinsic remainders, the joined remainder is exactly

 R_join=R_left+kR_right+delta||W-Ap||^2.                  (13)

Now fix m>=128 and, for this WHOLE horizon, set

 sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),
 r_j=a c^j, M_j=1+sum H_j,
 t_j=r_j/M_j, delta_j=2-(1+t_j)^2.                       (14)

The parameters are not recomputed inside the recursion. Counting gives

 M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,
 A_mass=a/(c-2),
 t_j=(c-2)/[1+B(2/c)^j],
 B=(1-A_mass)/A_mass
   =[(sigma^2+2)m+sigma^2]/[m(m-1)].                    (15)

For m>=128, 0<B<8/m, 2/c<5/6 and 0<c-2<1/2. The first estimate follows
from sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)<35/6 and m>83; the others follow from
12/5<sigma<17/7 and m>=128. For j=m-2 or m-1, we have j>=12 and
(2/c)^j<(5/6)^12<1/8. Using 1/(1+x)>=1-x in (15) gives

 t_j>(c-2)-(c-2)/m
     =(sigma-2)+[sigma-(c-2)]/m
     >(sigma-2)+1/m.

Therefore delta_j<-2/m for these two levels.

Use the actual three-block cuts

                   k=2^(m-1), l=3*2^(m-2).             (16)

Write U=M_(m-1), V=M_(m-2), r=r_(m-1), s=r_(m-2).
Set all gradients on the three blocks to respectively

        u0=-(V+s)/(U+r) v0,       v0,       0,           (17)

where v0 is any nonzero scalar or vector. The full step displacement is
D=sum h_i g_i=(U+r)u0+(V+s)v0=0, and g_n=0. All node squares in (13)
vanish except those at the root and its right child: every smaller node
has constant gradients. Thus

 R_Hm=M_m^2 { delta_(m-1)(V+s)^2/(U+r)^2
                             +delta_(m-2)V^2/U^2 }||v0||^2
      < -8 M_m^2/(25m) ||v0||^2.                       (18)

For the last inequality use both negative deltas and U/V=2+t_(m-2)<5/2.

The obstruction survives the whole following objective conversion family.
For any 0<alpha<=1/S with S=M_m-1, multiply the intrinsic array by alpha,
add lambda_(i,star)=2alpha h_i for i<n, add
lambda_(n,star)=2beta with beta=1-alpha S>=0, and take ANY q. Every
coefficient is nonnegative and (1) holds. Direct expansion gives

 C_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,alpha D+beta g_n>
          +alpha||D||^2+2beta<D,g_n>
          +(alpha S M_m+beta)||g_n||^2+alpha R_Hm.       (19)

To check (19), expand 2sum h_i Q_(i,star)-V_h as
||D||^2-2<x_0,D>+2S f_n+S M_m||g_n||^2, and then add 2beta Q_(n,star).
The values cancel because alpha S+beta=1. On (17), set x_0=0; then
(19)=alpha R_Hm<0 independently of q. In particular this array does NOT
pass A_2 at q=1/M_m. Its failure is quantitative and occurs in the already
requested collection, with actual moments and full original flows.

The vector (17) is not a legal adversary: it is a negative test direction
for this particular coefficient family. Only a proof excluding EVERY
nonnegative common array, together with (9), would produce the claimed
legal lower obstruction. No such exclusion has been established here.

## 7. Remaining decision and next interface

The task's central dichotomy remains open. Section 5 converts it exactly to
an attained legal performance problem on a specified proper Gram cone;
Section 6 rejects an explicit scalable candidate at every q. Neither gives
the common feasible-array formula or the collection-wide lower bound at
1/M_m that the assignment requests.

Adding all four-interval partitions would test every three-jump principal
block in the common matrix (4). It adds the determinant/correlation excluded
by the explicit example after (5), without yet reverting to arbitrary
singleton PEP. This names the new constraint precisely; it is not a claim
that those extra partitions are sufficient for the quantitative target.
The exact all-schedule F/G comparison and the original target are unchanged.

## 8. General strict feasibility: equality (9) extends to every h>=0

The restriction h_i>1 in Section5 can be removed. Define the collection for
compressed horizons n<=2 to contain the entire original formal space; this
is exactly the condition of at most two chronological jumps at those horizons.
With that convention, (9), primal attainment and common-array attainment hold
for EVERY finite nonnegative schedule. Here is an explicit proof.

First assume every h_i>0 and n>=1. Write h_min=min_i h_i, S=sum h_i>0, and
choose the following strictly positive scalar:

 theta=min{1/4, 1/(4S), min(h_min^2,1)/[64S^2(S+1)]}.     (20)

Use the scalar quadratic with curvature theta as a starting transcript:

 X_i=product_(t<i)(1-theta h_t),
 gamma_i=theta X_i, f_i=theta X_i^2/2, X_0=1.

All X_i lie in [1/2,1]. Indeed every factor is positive, and induction gives
product_(t<i)(1-theta h_t)>=1-theta P_i>=3/4. The weaker 1/2 bound suffices.
The scalar increments are

 d_i=gamma_i-gamma_(i-1)=-theta^2 h_(i-1) X_(i-1)<0,
 eta_i=|d_i|sum_(j!=i)|d_j|, 1<=i<=n.                    (21)

Enlarge the space to have orthonormal coordinates e_0,e_1,...,e_n and set

 x_0=e_0,
 g_i=gamma_i e_0+sum_(k<=i)sqrt(eta_k)e_k,
 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.                              (22)

Keep the scalar values f_i unchanged. The Gram matrix is PSD because the
vectors are explicit. Its incremental Gram matrix is

                   D=d d^T+diag(eta_1,...,eta_n).        (23)

This matrix has an explicit two-index PSD decomposition: on each pair i<j
insert [[|d_i d_j|,d_i d_j],[d_i d_j,|d_i d_j|]], and on coordinate i
insert the remaining singleton d_i^2. Thus the Gram matrix of (22) lies
in K_2 by the exact marginal criterion in Section4. That criterion was
proved directly there and is part of this same candidate proof.

Every original interpolation residual remains STRICTLY negative. To check
it quantitatively, the scalar quadratic has, for finite i!=j,

 Q_ij^base=-theta(1-theta)(X_i-X_j)^2/2.

For i<j,
X_i-X_j=theta sum_(i<=t<j) h_t X_t
        >=theta(P_j-P_i)/2>=theta h_min/2.
Since theta<=1/4, it follows that

 Q_ij^base<=-theta^3 h_min^2/16.

Both optimum residuals of this quadratic satisfy
Q_(i,star)^base=Q_(star,i)^base
=-theta(1-theta)X_i^2/2<=-theta/16.

On the added coordinates, sum eta_i<=theta^4 S^2. Consequently each added
gradient has norm at most theta^2 S, and each added position has norm at
most theta^2 S^2. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the absolute added contribution
to any finite or optimum interpolation residual by

                         2theta^4 S^2(S+1).             (24)

For finite pairs, (20) makes (24) at most
theta^3 h_min^2/32. For optimum pairs it is at most theta/32: use theta<=1
and 2theta^3 S^2(S+1)<=2theta S^2(S+1)<=1/32. These estimates leave every
residual strictly negative, with the original common values f_i. The
coordinate components need not be individually legal; their joint residuals
have just been checked in full.

Scale the vectors and values to give strict radius, and add a sufficiently
small interior K_2 Gram matrix as in Section5. This supplies the missing
strict feasible point for every positive schedule, however small its steps.
The compactness and explicit separation proof in Section5 now proves (9)
and both attainments for every positive schedule.

For zero steps, compress to the chronological positive-step indices i_0,
...,i_(p-1), retaining original n as the terminal representative. The
interpolation constraints at two identical positions force identical
gradients, since their summed residual is ||g_i-g_j||^2; the individual
residuals then force identical values. Deleting such repetitions preserves
the coordinate property of at most two jumps, and duplicating them in an
existing Gram realization also preserves it. Hence w_2 for the original
and compressed schedules is the same, and primal attainment lifts.

Take an attained common array for the positive compression and embed its
coefficients on the original representative indices, setting all other
coefficients zero. Every original value flow is correct. Each embedded Q
has precisely its compressed polynomial after selecting those gradients,
because all omitted steps have coefficient zero. An original two-jump
gradient list remains a two-jump list on the representative subsequence.
The embedded array consequently passes every original test and attains the
same q. This argument takes place on the original independent formal
gradient space and does not impose duplicate-gradient identities in the
dual. If there are no positive steps, w_2=1 and lambda_(n,star)=2 supplies
the full identity ||x_0-g_n||^2 at q=1.

The explicit orthogonal point (10) is a strict-feasibility device, not a
quantitative lower witness at the requested scale. More generally, accepted
fact `0e7e78056dc6258c` bounds EVERY legal orthogonal-gradient trajectory by
2/n^2. On the actual critical family, M_m<=c^m, n=2^m-1 and c<5/2 for
m>=128. Hence M_m times this whole subclass's normalized error is at most
2c^m/(2^m-1)^2, which tends to zero. A useful legal K_2 obstruction must
use correlations; strict feasibility alone cannot establish it.
