<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003235 -->
# Full Silver calibration and whole-array continuation tests

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. No fact_submit or numerical optimization.
This does not establish the original exact profile converse or a separation.
Every mathematical assertion below is proved in this draft; no unverified
shared finding is used as a correctness premise. The accepted interpolation
and full-certificate framework is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`.

The source starting point is Altschuler--Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize
Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization*,
arXiv:2309.16530 (2023), equations (3.1)--(3.9), Theorem 3.4 and its proof.
Its C>=0 convention is C_ij=-2Q_ji in the present notation. The exact local
PDF and extracted text were read. We reconstruct the needed identities,
including the complete objective and remainder, instead of importing a
distance-only statement. The generic skew-completion idea has an earlier
intrinsic-potential analogue in shared candidate666b5b9b14038a0f; the new
tests here concern actual full objective certificates and the assigned path.

## 1. Exact coefficients at every depth

Set rho=1+sqrt(2), n_j=2^j-1, and define the undeformed word by

 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+rho^(j-1),H_j).

Its mass is M_j=1+sum H_j=rho^j, since M_(j+1)=2M_j+rho^(j-1).
Normalize smoothness to1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All formal vectors
x_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent. Put

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 C_ij=-2Q_ji=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.

Here is one finite-node array L^(j), without optimum entries. At depth0 it
is zero. In a join let p=n_j, q=2n_j+1, M=rho^j, d=M/rho, and b=1+d.
Embed L^(j) on the left and rho^2 L^(j) on the right. Let right weights
w_i=h_i for p<i<q and w_q=1. For every p<i<=q add rho w_i to L_(p,i)
and to L_(q,i), omitting the diagonal entry L_(q,q). Add M to L_(q,p).
All coefficients are explicitly nonnegative. Repeated contributions add.

Define

 J_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
                       +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.

The full finite identity is

 J_j=sum_(i!=k)L^(j)_ik C_ik+M_j(M_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2.       (1)

Here is an induction with every nontrivial cancellation displayed. It is
zero=zero at j=0. In a join write g=g_p, e=g_q,
P=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i, W=P+e, and S=M-1. The quadratic parts of the two
weighted fans, before multiplication by rho, are respectively

 sum_i w_i C_(p,i): -M||g||^2-2d<g,W>-||W||^2
                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,
 sum_i w_i C_(q,i):  ||W||^2-M||e||^2
                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2.

The quadratic part of C_(q,p) is
(1+2d)||g||^2-||e||^2+2<g,W>.
These formulas follow by expanding the definitions and using
2sum_i<h_i g_i,sum_(p<t<i)h_t g_t>=||P||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.
Thus the added fans and M C_(q,p) have zero g,W cross coefficient
because M=rho d. Their g and e diagonal coefficients are
M(1+2d-rho) and -(rho+1)M. Their interior diagonal coefficients are
2rho h_i(h_i-1), exactly the difference between the parent and
rho^2 times the right child. Their value coefficients are

 f_p: 2M(rho-1)=2(S+b),
 f_q: 2rho(M-2)+2M=2[(rho^2-2)S-b],
 f_i, p<i<q: -4rho h_i=2(1-rho^2)h_i.

They therefore equal all value coefficients of J_(j+1)-J_left-rho^2 J_right.
After using both child identities, the g diagonal left over is

 M(M-1)-d(1+d)-M(1+2d-rho)=0,

and the e diagonal is
rho^2 M(M-1)+(rho+1)M=(rho M)(rho M-1).
This proves (1) at every depth.

Write M=M_m, S=M-1, v=2M-1. A sharp full objective array in the current
Q convention is

 Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/v for finite i!=k,
 Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/v for i<n,
 Lambda_(n,star)=2M/v,
 Lambda_(star,i)=0 for all finite i.

Its COMPLETE certificate, not just a primitive consequence, is

 (1/v)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik
                         =||x_n-Mg_n||^2/v.                 (2)

To check this multiply by v and use (1). The query optimum entries give
2sum h_i f_i-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i||g_i||^2. The terminal optimum
entry gives2M f_n-2M<g_n,x_n>+M||g_n||^2. The finite array gives
-J_m+M(M-1)||g_n||^2. All values cancel because v=M+S. The remaining
query terms telescope by
||x_0||^2-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i^2||g_i||^2=||x_n||^2.
The terminal terms complete the displayed square. This verifies every
original formal-space coefficient. The scalar Huber function with slope
1/v, quadratic core radius1/v, and x_0=1 attains2f_n=1/v: its iterates
are x_i=1-(sum_(t<i)h_t)/v>=M/v>1/v. Thus (2) is tight.

The actual canonical AP objective certificate is different. Define

 t=(1+sqrt(4M^2-3))/2, so t^2-t+1=M^2.

In the C convention its finite coefficients are the SAME L^(m), and its
optimum entries are C_(star,i):h_i for i<n, C_(star,n):t,
C_(n,star):S, with the other C_(i,star) coefficients zero. In Q notation,

 Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/t,
 Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/t (i<n), Lambda_(n,star)=2,
 Lambda_(star,n)=2S/t, Lambda_(star,i)=0 (i<n).

All are nonnegative, and the exact full identity is

 (1/t)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik
                         =||x_n-tg_n||^2/t.                 (3)

Indeed (1) and the same norm telescope give the C sum
||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2+2t<x_n,g_n>-2t f_n
                         -(M^2+t-1)||g_n||^2.
Since M^2+t-1=t^2 this is the required identity. The source paper uses
f_n<=r_m||x_0||^2 with r_m=1/(2t); our normalized objective coefficient
is 2r_m=1/t. No factor2 is suppressed.

Both (2) and (3) have rank-one complete remainder. Their kernels are,
respectively, x_0=sum h_i g_i+M g_n and x_0=sum h_i g_i+t g_n. The
kernel dimension is n+1 in scalar formal coordinates. These are actual
all-depth baseline arrays, with different objective constants and optimum
incidence; they must not be identified with one another.

## 2. Full coordinates and exact singular conditions

Let N=n+1, e the vector of N ones, e_n the last coordinate, and
V=I+T with T_it=h_t for t<i. For an arbitrary real N by N array A put

 b=Ae, a=A^T e-2e_n,
 G=sym(AV)-e_n e_n^T, sym(D)=(D+D^T)/2.

The actual multipliers are Lambda_ij=-A_ij (i!=j), Lambda_(i,star)=b_i,
Lambda_(star,i)=a_i. All actual signs and flows hold precisely when

 A_ij<=0 (i!=j), b>=0, a>=0.                              (4)

Expanding Q, or first canceling its value coefficients by these flows,
gives the complete matrix

 Mcert(q,A,h)=[[q,-b^T/2],[-b/2,G]].                       (5)

At either baseline put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),tau), where tau=M or t,
and q0=1/v or1/t, as appropriate. Make the invertible fixed-coordinate
substitution x_0=z+w0^T g. At any nearby parameters the matrix becomes

 [[q,r^T],[r,D]],
 r=q w0-b/2,
 D=G+q w0w0^T-(w0b^T+bw0^T)/2.

For q>0 its PSD condition is EXACTLY

                    D-r r^T/q >=0.                     (6)

At the baseline r=D=0. If q,A,h are analytic in s, write
r=s r1+s^2 r2+..., D=s D1+s^2 D2+.... Then D1>=0 is necessary, but
on ker D1 one also needs D2-r1 r1^T/q0>=0. At any subsequent singular
block [[B,C],[C^T,E]], the exact conditions are B>=0,
range(C) contained in range(B), and E-C^T B^dagger C>=0. Here B^dagger
inverts B on its range and is zero on its kernel. To see the range
condition, a vector in ker B must have zero mixed product with every
other vector under a PSD form; completing squares on range B then gives
the last condition. Thus there is no license to discard mixed blocks.

At an initially zero A_ij, a one-sided feasible derivative requires
dot A_ij<=0; at an initially zero component of a or b, the corresponding
derivative must be nonnegative. Positive coefficients allow signed
variations only while their FINAL values satisfy (4).

## 3. An explicit dense rank-one transport, with no unknown PSD

Here we attempt continuation from the sharp baseline (2). The chosen
physical path is exactly the assignment, with epsilon=s/m:

 c=rho(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
 H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), j<m.

Let h be that word, M=1+sum h, v=2M-1, q=1/v, and
p=V^(-1)e. Thus p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i.
In a neighborhood of epsilon=0 define

 mu=1-sqrt(1-v p_n^2),
 wphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M),
 delta=(mu-wphys^T p)/h_0,
 w=wphys+delta(e_0-e_1).                                (7)

This is analytic at zero, since there 1-v p_n^2=(M-1)^2/M^2>0,
and delta(0)=0. It satisfies e^T w=v and w^T p=mu. All w_i are
positive sufficiently near zero. For n=1 the second coordinate is the
terminal; the formula still applies.

Put S0=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T and define

 U=[e,p], H=U^T U,
 R=[e_n+w-2V^T e_n, q(2-mu)w-p_n e_n],
 P=U H^(-1)U^T,
 Kpart=R H^(-1)U^T-U H^(-1)R^T
                             -U H^(-1)(U^T R)H^(-1)U^T.

The two columns of U are independent because h_0>0. Directly,
U^T R is skew: its first diagonal uses sum w=v=1+2sum h, the second
uses q mu(2-mu)=p_n^2, and its mixed entries sum to zero using Vp=e.
It follows by multiplication that Kpart is skew and Kpart U=R.

At the undeformed baseline let A0 be the exact array from (2), V0 its
V, and K0=(A0 V0-V0^T A0^T)/2. Define the ACTUAL proposed full array

 K=Kpart+(I-P)K0(I-P),  A=(S0+K)V^(-1).                  (8)

There is no support deletion in (8); it generally creates dense interior
entries. It preserves A(0)=A0. Indeed every skew matrix with KU=R has
the form Kpart+(I-P)Omega(I-P), where Omega is arbitrary skew, because
the difference annihilates U on both sides. Taking Omega=K0 recovers
K0 at zero by this decomposition. Also

 Ae=2q w, A^T e=2e_n, sym(AV)=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T.

Consequently the FULL matrix (5) is identically
q[1;-w][1;-w]^T. All nullspace, mixed-block and range requirements hold
exactly. Optimum signs hold locally because w>0 and a=0. The remaining
indispensable condition is A_ij<=0 for EVERY distinct pair. It is not
implied by the identities just proved.

## 4. Exact sign failure, including all free skew repairs at three steps

For m=2 write r=sqrt(2). At epsilon=0, h=(r,2,r), M=3+2r,
v=5+4r. The baseline A0, with all entries divided by7, is

 [[6-2r, 10-8r, 0, 0],
  [-6+2r, -8+12r, 0, -6+2r],
  [0, 4-6r, 10+6r, 2-10r],
  [0, -6+2r, -10-6r, 18+8r]].

The physical derivative with respect to epsilon is
dot h=(-2/rho,-1,-2/rho). Formula (7) gives dot delta=1-4r.
Differentiating the EXPLICIT formula (8), using only product and inverse
rules, gives

 dot A_02=(-302+467r)/98,
 dot A_30=(-187+221r)/49.                               (9)

For reproducibility K0, with all entries divided by7, is

 [[0,-6+2r,10-8r,-4-r],
  [6-2r,0,-12+4r,-8-2r],
  [-10+8r,12-4r,0,-2-11r],
  [4+r,8+2r,2+11r,0]].

The values (9) can thus be checked by substituting into (7)--(8),
with d(X^(-1))=-X^(-1)(dX)X^(-1). The first value is positive, since
sqrt(2)>1 and467>302. Because A0_02=0, formula (8) makes the actual
multiplier Lambda_02=-A_02 negative for every sufficiently small positive
epsilon. This is an exact sign counterexample to this proposed transport.

More strongly, keeping the boundary weights (7), NO choice of the free
skew part can repair this derivative at depth2. At zero put

 u=(r-2,1-r,1,0)^T,
 z=(2r-3,2-2r,0,1)^T.

They span the orthogonal complement of e,p, where
p=(1,1-r,r-1,2r-3)^T. Hence all alternative skew first derivatives,
while retaining the same A0 and dot w, differ by gamma(u z^T-z u^T).
After multiplication by V0^(-1), their changes at entries02 and30 are
respectively gamma and -r gamma. Therefore every such derivative obeys

 r dot A_02+dot A_30=(40+10r)/7>0.                       (10)

Both baseline entries are zero, so both derivatives would need to be
nonpositive. Equation (10) rules this out. This test permits EVERY finite
interpolation pair and every free skew direction for the specified w.
It does NOT exclude changing w differently, creating a nonzero PSD
remainder, relaxing q, or choosing another baseline certificate.

The accompanying tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) audit checks all value flows,
complete matrices and derivatives at one and three steps. It is an
arithmetic audit, not a solver and not a correctness authority; the
displayed exact matrices and formulas are the mathematical evidence.

## 5. All-depth stiffness of any rank-one continuation

There is a separate quantitative necessity that does not fix the two-weight
choice (7) or the free skew part. It describes rank-one continuation at the
sharp Huber rate, not arbitrary PSD continuations.

At depth m let N=2^m and M0=rho^m. For an integer i let pop(i) be the
number of ones in its binary expansion. On the undeformed Silver word,

 p_i=(-1)^i rho^(-pop(i)),
 dot p_i=-(i+pop(i))p_i,                                (11)

where dots denote derivatives in epsilon=s/m at zero. To verify this,
sum_(t=1)^i nu_2(t)=i-pop(i): summing floor(i/2^k) over k gives the
left side and summing the binary expansion gives the right side.
The product of h_t-1 therefore has exponent -pop(i). Under the physical
path the logarithmic derivative of h_t-1 is nu_2(t+1)-2, whose sum is
-i-pop(i). This proves (11), including p_n=-1/M0 and
dot p_n=(N+m-1)/M0.

Let D=rho^2+2=2rho+3. The physical mass has exact formula

 M(epsilon)=A(epsilon)c^m+(1-A(epsilon))2^m,
 A(epsilon)=a/(c-2).

Here A(0)=1, dot A(0)=-D, and hence

                    dot M=M0(m-D)+D N.                (12)

Suppose ANY differentiable full certificate, with every pair available,
has q=1/(2M(epsilon)-1), a rank-one remainder q||x_0-w^Tg||^2, and
starts at (2). No multiplier formula or support pattern is assumed.
On the actual full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, all Q_ij are zero,
x_0=1 and g_i=p_i. Thus

 q(1-w^T p)^2=q-p_n^2.

The baseline selects the analytic minus branch, so w^T p=mu in (7).
Let dot wphys=(dot h_0,...,dot h_(n-1),dot M), and let
zeta=dot w-dot wphys. Differentiating the preceding scalar equality gives

 p^T zeta=(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(M0-1)=:Delta_m.             (13)

For detail, dot mu=[dot M-(2M0-1)(N+m-1)]/[M0(M0-1)].
Also wphys^T p=1+(M-1)p_n, whose derivative is
-dot M/M0+(M0-1)(N+m-1)/M0. Subtracting gives (13).

Tightness at q=1/(2M-1) additionally forces every optimum-source
multiplier to vanish: on the attaining Huber trajectory every Q_(star,i)
is strictly negative, while all finite Q and all Q_(i,star) are zero.
The PSD certificate must have zero value there, so complementarity gives
this assertion. Then A^T e=2e_n and sum w=2M-1, whence sum zeta=0.
Since max_i p_i=1 and min_i p_i=-1/rho, this also yields

 sum_i |zeta_i| >=sqrt(2)|Delta_m|.                    (14)

Indeed subtract the midpoint of the range of p before applying the
triangle inequality, and use sum zeta=0. The two-weight derivative (7)
attains (14), so its failure in Section4 is not caused by excessive
total redistribution in this particular norm.

More significantly, put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M0). Since all h_i>1,
h_i|p_i|=|p_i|+|p_(i+1)|. Also
sum_(i=0)^n |p_i|=(1+1/rho)^m=(sqrt(2))^m. Therefore

 sum_i (w0)_i |p_i|=2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0.

It follows that every such rank-one continuation satisfies

 max_i |zeta_i|/(w0)_i
       >= |Delta_m|/[2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0].               (15)

By (12), Delta_m/N tends to -1 as m tends to infinity. Consequently,
in the original s parameter the right side of (15) is asymptotic to

                         (sqrt(2))^m/(2m).            (16)

Thus no horizon-uniform bound on the componentwise relative FIRST
derivative of this rank-one boundary vector is possible, even with the
complete coefficient array available. Natural physical weights have
bounded relative s derivatives at zero, so the same divergence cannot
be hidden by merely counting their physical variation.

This is NOT a bound on total finite-path change: an analytic path may
have a very thin boundary layer. It is NOT an obstruction to PSD
remainders of higher rank. Such a remainder can absorb the quadratic
probe, so (13) need no longer hold as an equality.

The same phenomenon occurs at the actual canonical source baseline (3).
Keep q(epsilon)=1/t(epsilon), t^2-t+1=M(epsilon)^2, and assume the
complete remainder remains rank one starting at (3). Set
wphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),t). The quadratic calibration again forces
w^T p=1-sqrt(1-t p_n^2). Differentiation gives

 p^T(dot w-dot wphys)
                   =(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(t0-1),         (17)

where t0=(1+sqrt(4M0^2-3))/2. To check, use
dot t=2M0 dot M/(2t0-1),
sqrt(1-t0/M0^2)=(t0-1)/M0,
and (t0-1)^2+t0=M0^2. The weighted denominator is now
2(sqrt(2))^m-1+(t0-1)/M0, so the same asymptotic lower bound (16)
holds. This calculation allows both directions of optimum incidence;
it uses only the rank-one full remainder and the specified rate.

## 6. A complete higher-rank analytic repair, with unaffordable exact cost

There is no qualitative obstruction to analytic continuation when the
objective coefficient is unrestricted. The following construction gives
an explicit full-array path from EITHER baseline, at every m>=2, with
all actual pairs and both optimum directions strictly positive for s>0.
Its objective cost is far too large. This is a quantified failed repair,
not the uniform rate conclusion requested in TASK.

Use N=2^m, n=N-1, M0=rho^m, and set

 Hmax=1+3M0, Lmax=6M0, R=(1+4Hmax)^2,
 d_i=8R^(n-i), D=diag(d_i), Qsafe=4sum_i d_i,
 eta0=1/[2N(1+n Hmax)],
 Asafe=D+eta0(NI-ee^T).

All physical h_i(s) lie below Hmax, and |h_i(s)-h_i(0)|<=Lmax s.
For the latter, differentiate
rho^(j-1)(1-s/m)(1+s/m)^(j-1), j=0,...,m-1. The j=0 derivative is
at most2/(m rho) in magnitude. For j>=1 its magnitude is at most
(j/m)rho^(j-1)(1+1/m)^(j-1)<=3M0. Both asserted bounds follow.
The bound (1+1/m)^m<3 follows by expanding the binomial and using
binom(m,k)/m^k<=1/k! and sum_(k>=0)1/k!<3.

First omit the eta0 term. The gradient block is sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T.
After congruence by D^(-1/2), its off-diagonal entries have absolute
values at most (Hmax/2)R^(-|i-j|/2). The sum of absolute off-diagonal
entries in each row is at most Hmax/(sqrt(R)-1)=1/4. Its diagonal is1,
except the terminal value1-1/8=7/8. The inequality
2|z_i z_j|<=z_i^2+z_j^2 therefore proves

 sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T >= D/4.

For every scalar z and gradient vector g, weighted Cauchy--Schwarz gives

 Qsafe z^2-z sum_i d_i g_i+g^T[sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T]g
 >=(Qsafe/2)z^2+[1/4-(sum_i d_i)/(2Qsafe)]sum_i d_i g_i^2
 >=z^2+sum_i g_i^2.

Here Qsafe=4sum d_i and min d_i=8. Thus the complete matrix before
the eta0 term is at least the identity, including its initial row.
The eta0 term has zero row and column sums, so it changes only the
gradient block by eta0 sym((NI-ee^T)V). Its norm is at most
eta0 N(1+n Hmax)=1/2: NI-ee^T has norm N, and
||V||<=1+||T||_F<=1+n Hmax. Consequently

                    Mcert(Qsafe,Asafe,h(s)) >= I/2.     (18)

Its actual finite multipliers are all eta0>0. Its optimum multipliers
are b_i=d_i and a_i=d_i-2*1_(i=n), all strictly positive. This proves
the full signs and PSD directly, without an appeal to Slater theory.

Let A0,q0 be either FULL baseline in Section1, and let
C_A=sum_(i,j)|A0_ij|, K=2 C_A N Lmax. Define

 A(s)=[A0+K s Asafe]/(1+K s),
 q(s)=[q0+K s Qsafe]/(1+K s).                            (19)

The physical h(s) stays the assigned word. The frozen baseline array
changes its full matrix by a gradient block sym(A0(T(s)-T(0))). Its
norm is at most C_A N Lmax s=K s/2. Hence

 Mcert(q0,A0,h(s))>=-(K s/2)I.

Convexly combining this with (18) proves FULL PSD of (19) at every
s in[0,1]. All final multiplier signs hold by convex combination, and
every pair is positive for s>0. The matrix and array are real analytic;
the baseline singular kernel and all mixed directions are included in
the displayed bounds. Everything is predetermined by m and s.

The exact cost explains why this does not solve the research task.
C_A>=sum b_i>=2 for either baseline, so K tends to infinity. For every
fixed s>0, the fraction K s/(1+K s) eventually exceeds1/2. Therefore

 q(s)>=Qsafe/2>=16(1+4Hmax)^(2n).                       (20)

The resulting certificate level diverges rather than being O(1/M_m).
This specific dense repair is feasible but fails the scalar comparison
by an enormous margin. It rules out interpreting singularity of the
chosen baseline as an absolute obstruction to whole-array analytic
continuation; an obstruction relevant to TASK must be quantitative.
No optimality is claimed for (19), and its cost is not a lower bound on
other higher-rank repairs.

## 7. What remains open

The canonical full baseline has now been calibrated at every depth, and
the sharper full baseline is separately explicit. The dense proposal
(7)--(8) solves its full PSD and all mixed-block conditions identically,
but its ACTUAL coefficient signs fail. Even arbitrary free skew changes
cannot fix its prescribed boundary-weight derivative at three steps.
Any rank-one continuation at either displayed rate also has the growing
relative-derivative requirement (16). These are scoped obstructions,
not a claim that every optimal certificate has either shape.

The explicit higher-rank path (19) uses every such sign direction and
is feasible at every depth, but its actual objective coefficient (20)
is unaffordable. The assignment permits other higher-rank PSD remainders,
different boundary weights, nonzero optimum sources, and a relaxed q.
Those freedoms are not excluded here. To obtain a separation, one must control the FINAL
signs and the exact finite-path Schur remainder uniformly in m, with
limsup q_m M_m<e/rho at s=1 (or the corresponding strict fixed-s gap).
Neither a derivative, the rank-one scalar moment, nor the real-skew
parameterization supplies that remaining estimate. No such certificate
or unrestricted obstruction has been proved in this round.

# Rotating three-group simplex faces with the entire carrier price

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF ATTEMPT.** Worker max2, project
gd-exact-profile-20260926, allocated invocation after ended69, ceiling70 /
attempted71. The formal gate is closed. None of the new mathematics below is
a fact, and none is used as an independently accepted intermediate premise.
There is one substantive mechanism: a maximal-support inverse on a genuine
two-dimensional triangular face. No second mechanism or renewal is opened.

The construction has three participating upper groups and changing support
identities. In the final five original queries of the diagnostic word its
supports include the two different edges {1,3} and {1,2}, separated by the
full triangle. Three actual gradients are noncollinear. Thus this is not the
previous fixed face barycenter plus one scalar Huber direction.

The new whole-law conclusion is a **scoped obstruction**, not a gain witness.
For all parameters in the law defined below, on every SAME-enclosing-m
H_(m,1/4), m>=1536, its actual normalized terminal score E obeys

    E <= 1/(D_m-8),       B_(2^m-1) E < 19/20.               (A)

The family contains the attained score 1/D_m. Consequently its entire
supremum is between 1/D_m and 1/(D_m-8), and any relative improvement over
the COMPLETE K/ray lower bounds is at most D_m/(D_m-8). That factor tends
to one. No exact domination E<=K_n or E<=F_ray_n is claimed.

The scope is explicit: three equal-sized upper groups of size r>=1, q>=r
remaining coordinates, and terminal transverse point anywhere in the
closed centered triangle. The common-direction price is included in the
same static function's OWN radius. Small inactive fractions q<r, arbitrary
terminal transverse points outside that triangle, arbitrary simplex starts,
and arbitrary terminal completions are not ruled out.

## 1. Original target and accepted inputs

For every integer n>=0, L>0, and predetermined finite nonnegative word
h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), the original error is

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).

The supremum is over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex
differentiable function with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimizer
x_star, and every x_0!=x_star. Thus W=2R in the project's normalization.
The problem asks whether W_n^star=inf_(h>=0) W_n(h) satisfies

    lim_(n->infinity) B_n W_n^star=1.

An affirmative answer requires: for every epsilon>0 there is a finite
N_epsilon such that for EVERY n>=N_epsilon and EVERY nonnegative word h of
length n, W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. The same N_epsilon must work for all h.
A negative answer requires a genuine upper bound on the full function-class
supremum for an infinite sequence of actual words. This report does neither.
The complete original PROBLEM.md is preserved without modification.

Retain every original integer split:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,
    Gbench(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Gbench(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                             (1)

The following accepted facts are used only at the stated scopes:

* **931b9653ce0f7201**, read in full: C_n<=(n+1)^rho and
  B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho for every n with all splits, and B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50
  for the exact hierarchy below, every m>=1536. Its full-W implication is
  conditional. No full-function upper bound from that fact is assumed.
* **b98bb807d514e80d**, read in full: the complete K_n includes 1/(1+2S),
  where S=sum h. Its full definition is the maximum of that term and all
  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)].
* **1fca3e5dd555cb4a**, statement read: K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n, where F_ray_n
  retains all feasible alternating-ray checkpoint choices and their remote
  offsets and areas. No evaluation of F_ray_n on this hierarchy is used.
* **1252b8823bb5a290**, statement and interpolation-necessity proof read:
  the original ordered-row convention. All rows needed for the present
  static function are also established directly below; its terminal is not
  replaced by a maximal completion.

The previous reports 2993fddcc4a03577 and f7f9930b98aa7caa are awareness
only. Their inverse, metric, and obstruction claims are not premises.

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536 and use this SAME m at every child:

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum h, D_m=1+2S.                       (2)

Every actual h_i>1; no original leaf, bridge, or terminal is discarded.
The finite law below is defined more generally for every finite word with
h_i>1, including the empty word. It does not purport to cover arbitrary
nonnegative words. The original every-n/every-h question remains separate.

## 2. Fixed real slopes, function, and complete minimum set

Choose integers r>=1 and q>=r; both may depend on the whole word. Put
N=3r+q. Partition the original N coordinates into three groups G_1,G_2,G_3
of size r and a remaining set I of size q. Choose any real sigma>0. Write
u_N=(1/N,...,1/N) and use the Euclidean subspace

    E_N={x in R^N: sum_l x_l=0}.

There are exactly N fixed real slopes, all offsets zero:

    s_l=sigma(e_l-u_N), 1<=l<=N,
    D=conv{s_l:1<=l<=N},
    F(x)=max_(g in D) [<x,g>-||g||^2/2], x in E_N.           (3)

Their exact Gram matrix is

    <s_l,s_k>=sigma^2(1_(l=k)-1/N).                          (4)

The function does not change during the run. The maximization in (3) is
over the entire convex hull, not merely over the original vertices. The
original affine dictionary is max_l <s_l,x>, with all its offsets zero;
(3) is the fixed quadratic smoothing used throughout this report.

Existence of a maximizing g follows from compactness of D; strict concavity
of the quadratic gives uniqueness. Completing the square identifies g as
the Euclidean projection of x onto D. Its exact condition is

    <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in D.                            (5)

For two inputs x,x', sum the two corresponding inequalities to obtain
<x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2. Hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||. Evaluating (3) at
each other's maximizers gives a nonnegative first-order remainder bounded
above by <x'-x,g'-g>, hence by ||x'-x||^2. Therefore F is differentiable,
convex, finite everywhere, and grad F=g is 1-Lipschitz. Convexity also
follows directly by taking the supremum of the affine functions in (3).

Zero is in the relative interior of D in E_N. Indeed a centered vector w
with every w_l> -sigma/N belongs to D because w/sigma+u_N has positive
entries summing to one. Thus for any x!=0, a sufficiently small t in (0,1)
satisfies tx in D and

    F(x)>=(t-t^2/2)||x||^2>0.

Since F(0)=0, the COMPLETE minimum set is exactly {0}. The smoothness
constant is exactly 1, since grad F(x)=x in a neighborhood of zero.
Set f=L F for general L>0. The original GD update then uses grad F and
the same original h. The OWN closest-minimum radius is ||x_0||.

The subspace E_N is an ordinary Euclidean space of dimension N-1, not a
variable metric. An explicit orthonormal basis is
(e_1+...+e_k-k e_(k+1))/sqrt(k(k+1)), 1<=k<N; its coordinates identify
(3) with a function on R^(N-1) without altering any inner product.

## 3. The two-dimensional transverse triangle and exact metric

Let U=G_1 union G_2 union G_3 and define the actual face barycenter

    c_face=sigma(1_U/(3r)-u_N),
    alpha^2=||c_face||^2=sigma^2 q/(3rN), b=sigma/sqrt(r).

Define T={z in R^3:z_1+z_2+z_3=0} and its centered triangle

    K=conv{e_j-(1,1,1)/3:1<=j<=3}
     ={z in T:z_j>=-1/3 for every j}.

The isometry J:T->E_N puts z_j/sqrt(r) on every coordinate of G_j and
zero on I. It is orthogonal to c_face. Thus

    ||R c_face+b Jz||^2=alpha^2 R^2+b^2||z||^2,
    kappa=b^2/alpha^2=3N/q<=12.                              (6)

The last inequality uses exactly the law's count condition q>=r. It is not
an estimate valid for all possible group counts.

For z in T, let tau be the unique real number satisfying

    sum_(j=1)^3 (z_j+1/3-tau)_+=1.

Set p_j=(z_j+1/3-tau)_+ and v=p-(1,1,1)/3. At tau=0 the left side
is at least sum_j(z_j+1/3)=1, so tau>=0. The residual z-v has value tau
on every positive-p coordinate and at most tau on every zero-p coordinate,
and its sum is zero. Therefore <z-v,w-v><=0 for every w in K. This proves
v=P_K(z), including all boundary cases, without a projection black box.
Define

    Phi(z)=<z,v>-||v||^2/2.

The same calculation as (3)-(5) makes Phi a convex 1-smooth function with
gradient v. The residual identities give

    Phi(z)-||v||^2/2=<z-v,v>=tau.                            (7)

For z in K, v=z, tau=0 and 2Phi(z)=||z||^2<=2/3. The last bound follows
by convexity of squared norm and the squared norm 2/3 of each vertex.

For ANY R>=1 and ANY z in T, the full ambient simplex projection at

    x=R c_face+b Jz

is exactly

    g=c_face+b Jv.                                           (8)

To check against every original vertex, the active upper residual level is

    sigma[(R-1)q/(3rN)+tau/r],

and the residual on I is -sigma(R-1)/N. Their difference is
sigma[(R-1)/3+tau]/r>=0. Other upper residuals are no larger. Thus (5)
holds for all N original vertices, including those declared inactive. If
R>1 the residual inequality for I is strict. At R=1 equality is harmless:
the projection is still unique, and every coordinate in I still has zero
barycentric weight.

The full function value and height are therefore

    F(x)=alpha^2(R-1/2)+b^2 Phi(z),
    F(x)-||g||^2/2=alpha^2(R-1)+b^2 tau.                     (9)

This is a genuine two-dimensional triangle process plus the actual common
direction. It is not the earlier affine-plus-scalar-Huber decomposition.

## 4. A concrete finite inverse rule, with every threshold guard

Fix the entire finite word h with h_i>1 before choosing the instance.
Choose a terminal z_n anywhere in K and R>=1. Ties between equal coordinate
values below are broken by the fixed index order 1<2<3. No coordinates or
group copies are created during the construction or the forward run.

Proceed symbolically backwards in the original chronological indices. For
one original step h=1+d, d>0, and a known centered future vector y, sort
its coordinates y_(1)>=y_(2)>=y_(3), retaining their original identities.
Choose the set A by exactly this rule:

    if 3 y_(1)<d:             A={1,2,3};
    else if y_(1)-y_(2)<d:    A={indices of y_(1),y_(2)};
    otherwise:               A={index of y_(1)}.             (10)

All comparisons are exact real comparisons. Strict inequalities in the
first two branches fix the rule at equality too; no limiting preimage is
silently admitted. Write k=|A| and set

    tau=[sum_(j in A)y_j+d(1-k/3)]/k,
    z_j=(h tau-y_j)/d           for j in A,
    z_j=y_j-h/3                for j outside A.              (11)

For k=3, tau=0 and max y<d/3. For k=2, tau=(-y_(3)+d/3)/2>0 and

    tau+d/3=(y_(1)+y_(2)+d)/2>y_(1).

For k=1, tau=y_(1)+2d/3>0 and tau+d/3=y_(1)+d>y_(1), since y_(1)>=0.
Consequently in EVERY branch

    y_j<tau+d/3 for every coordinate j.                      (12)

For j in A, (11) gives z_j+1/3-tau=(tau+d/3-y_j)/d>0.
For j outside A it gives z_j+1/3-tau=y_j-(tau+d/3)<0.
The active positive parts sum to

    [k(tau+d/3)-sum_(j in A)y_j]/d=1.

Summing (11) gives sum_j z_j=0. Hence tau really is the threshold of z,
its exact active set is A, its projected gradient v has p_j>0 exactly on
A, and direct substitution yields

    z-h v=y.                                                 (13)

This proves ALL inverse guards, for every possible future vector, rather
than just the guards of the displayed short suffix. In particular it covers
all previously visited and all subsequently specified supports when the
finite inverse dictionary is traversed forward.

Apply (10)-(11) at i=n-1,...,0, with y=z_(i+1), h=h_i. Denote the resulting
thresholds and projected gradients by tau_i and v_i. At i=n take tau_n=0,
v_n=z_n, and p_n=z_n+(1,1,1)/3. Every z_i is finite because the word is
finite, every h_i is finite, and every d_i>0. This is a textual finite
selection rule; no recurrence or trajectory was executed by a program.

An explicit affine-product expression for every chosen initial coordinate
can also be given. Once A_i is fixed, let M_i be block diagonal with
active block -I/d_i+h_i 11^T/(k_i d_i), inactive identity block, and zero
cross blocks. Let (b_i^aff)_j=h_i(1/k_i-1/3) on A_i and -h_i/3 elsewhere.
Then

    z_i=M_i z_(i+1)+b_i^aff,
    z_0=(M_0...M_(n-1))z_n
        +sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(M_0...M_(i-1))b_i^aff.              (14)

An empty product is identity. This is not a product of independently chosen
best histories: every A_i is selected from its one already fixed future.
The formula is part of the same joint derivation, not a computed artifact.

Equal original coordinates cannot split in the forward run: the same
threshold map applies to each. Formula (13) also shows directly that any
equality in a constructed predecessor is retained in its prescribed future.
Distinct predecessors may merge. The fixed group sizes never change.

## 5. Every original physical point, row, Gram entry, and radius

Put S_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t, with S_n=0, and R_i=R+S_i. The initial point
and all its actual iterates are

    x_i=R_i c_face+b Jz_i, 0<=i<=n,
    g_i=c_face+b Jv_i,
    F_i=alpha^2(R_i-1/2)+b^2 Phi(z_i).                        (15)

In literal original coordinates, for l in G_j and l in I respectively,

    (x_i)_l=sigma[R_i q/(3rN)+(z_i)_j/r],
    (x_i)_l=-sigma R_i/N;
    (g_i)_l=sigma[(p_i)_j/r-1/N],
    (g_i)_l=-sigma/N.                                        (16)

The ambient threshold applied to (x_i)_l/sigma+1/N is

    theta_i=(R_i-1)q/(3rN)+tau_i/r.                          (17)

It gives positive-part barycentric entries (p_i)_j/r on G_j and zero on I.
The latter guard is exactly -[(R_i-1)/3+tau_i]/r<=0, strictly negative at
every queried i<n. The triangle active/inactive guards are (12). Thus the
whole known word fixes every original coordinate and every threshold.

Equations (13) and R_i-h_i=R_(i+1) prove
x_i-h_i g_i=x_(i+1) at each of the n original steps. At every queried index,
R_i>1, so (9) gives strictly positive height. This includes all leaves and
bridges, not just the five queries used to demonstrate rotation.

For the actual vectors, the complete Gram entries are

    <x_i,x_j>=alpha^2 R_i R_j+b^2<z_i,z_j>,
    <x_i,g_j>=alpha^2 R_i+b^2<z_i,v_j>,
    <g_i,g_j>=alpha^2+b^2<v_i,v_j>.                           (18)

In particular (18) specifies the full original formal-vector Gram on
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including the real terminal gradient. It is PSD because
the vectors in (16) are real vectors in one Euclidean space. No rank or
support restriction is imposed on a claimed full PEP upper certificate.

For ANY ordered pair of samples i,j, expand (3) at their actual gradients:

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                              (19)

The inequality is the normal condition (5) for sample j, applied to g_i in
D. Include the optimum sample x_star=g_star=F_star=0: then Q_(i,star)=0
and Q_(star,i)=-[alpha^2(R_i-1)+b^2 tau_i]<=0. All remote, reversed,
incoming and outgoing terminal rows are included. No prior row is replaced
by an expected face or an averaged group history.

Because argmin F={0}, the OWN squared radius is exactly

    R_init^2=||x_0||^2
             =alpha^2(S+R)^2+b^2||z_0||^2>0.                (20)

At the terminal z_n in K, equations (9) and (20) give the quantitative exact
terminal formula

    E=2F(x_n)/R_init^2
      =[2R-1+kappa||z_n||^2]
        /[(S+R)^2+kappa||z_0||^2].                          (21)

This is the original W=2R score of f=L F about its unique true minimum.
The entire initial transverse history is present in the denominator. The
common direction contributes alpha^2(S+R)^2; it has not been declared free.

## 6. Actual rotation on the final five original queries

For every diagnostic m>=1536 choose the concrete terminal point

    z_n=(a/4,0,-a/4).                                        (22)

It is strictly inside K. All r>=1, q>=r, sigma>0 and R>=1 are allowed;
for a single completely specified member one can take r=q=sigma=1 and
R=S+1. Earlier coordinates are determined by (10)-(14), not by a reset.

We first justify the coarse inequalities used in this finite symbolic suffix
calculation. Since epsilon<=1/6144<1/1000, beta>999/1000. The elementary
bounds 12/5<lambda<17/7 give c<(17/7)(1001/1000)<49/20. Therefore

    2/5<a<5/12,  beta>999/1000, beta^2>19/20,
    u:=1/(4beta)<13/50.                                      (23)

For the lower bound on a, beta/c>999/2450>2/5. The upper bound follows
from beta<1, c>lambda>12/5. The bounds for beta^2 and u follow by squaring
or multiplying positive rational quantities; they involve no numerical
evaluation of an orbit.

The last five original d_i=h_i-1 values are

    a, beta*c, a, beta, a,

in forward order, because H_m ends in H_3 and a*c=beta. Put
d_2=beta*c=beta^2/a. Starting from (22), the last two inverse steps use the
full-triangle branch and give

    z_(n-1)=(-1/4,0,1/4),
    z_(n-2)=(u,0,-u).                                        (24)

Their guards are 3a/4<a and 3/4<beta. At i=n-3, full support fails because
3u>3/4>a, but the top-two gap is u<a by (23). Thus A_(n-3)={1,2}, and

    tau=(u+a/3)/2, h=1+a,
    z^E:=z_(n-3)=(h/6+u(a-1)/(2a),
                         h/6+u(a+1)/(2a), -u-h/3),
    v_(n-3)=(1/6-u/(2a), 1/6+u/(2a), -1/3).                (25)

The associated positive barycentric weights are
1/2-u/(2a), 1/2+u/(2a), and zero. In particular both active weights are
strictly positive, and the edge is a real active face.

The entries of z^E have strict order z^E_2>z^E_1>z^E_3: the first
difference is u/a>0, while the second is

    z^E_1-z^E_3=h/2+u(3a-1)/(2a)>0.

At i=n-4 the full-triangle inverse is valid. Indeed

    3a z^E_2=(h/2)(3u+a)
       < (17/24)(39/50+5/12)
       =6103/7200<9/10<beta^2.

Thus 3 max z^E<d_2 and

    z_(n-4)=-z^E/d_2.                                       (26)

Its ordered coordinate identities are 3,1,2. For the next inverse at
i=n-5, full support fails since

    3(z_(n-4))_3=a(3u+h)/beta^2>a,

using 3u+h>1>beta^2. The top-two gap is

    (z^E_1-z^E_3)/d_2
      =[a h+u(3a-1)]/(2beta^2)<a.                            (27)

For the strict inequality, (23) and h<3/2 give
a(2beta^2-h)>(2/5)a>4/25, while
u(3a-1)<(13/50)(1/4)=13/200<4/25. Thus A_(n-5)={3,1}.

The actual support sequence at those five original queries is therefore

    {1,3}, {1,2,3}, {1,2}, {1,2,3}, {1,2,3}.                (28)

The terminal also has full support. Every group participates. In the
original N-coordinate simplex, a displayed group in (28) means all its r
actual copies are active, with the exact weight p_j/r from (16).

There is no scalar-line reduction: v_(n-2)=(u,0,-u) and
v_(n-1)=(-1/4,0,1/4) are distinct points on the line with second coordinate
zero, whereas the second coordinate of v_(n-3) in (25) is strictly positive.
These three transverse gradients are noncollinear. By the isometry J,
the same is true of the three ambient gradients after their common carrier
is restored. No fixed vector plus one fixed scalar direction contains them.

This is a symbolic proof for every m>=1536 using the actual suffix of the
same enclosing word. It is not a computed five-step replay or a substitute
for the full-word accounting in (20)-(21).

## 7. Whole-law comparison, including all earlier leaves and bridges

The proof of the obstruction requires no estimate that discards the initial
history. Instead it uses the nonnegative term that history contributes to
its OWN radius. For every allowed terminal and count choice put

    eta=kappa||z_n||^2, 0<=eta<=12*(2/3)=8.

Equation (21) gives

    E <= (2R-1+eta)/(S+R)^2.

For D_word=1+2S>eta, the exact completed-square identity is

    (S+R)^2-(D_word-eta)(2R-1+eta)
       =[R-(S+1-eta)]^2>=0.

The numerator is positive because R>=1. Therefore

    E <= 1/(D_word-eta) <= 1/(D_word-8) if D_word>8.          (29)

This is uniform over EVERY finite r,q with q>=r, every sigma>0, every R>=1,
every z_n in the closed K, every branch history selected by (10), and all
the resulting original initial points. Boundary terminals and exact inverse
threshold equalities are included. The bound is not merely local in m.

For z_n=0 the rule always selects all three coordinates, all z_i=0, and
choosing R=S+1 gives by (21) the attained score

    E=1/D_word.                                              (30)

For the rotating members (22), the more specific terminal charge is
eta=kappa a^2/8<=3a^2/2<25/96. Hence they even obey
E<=1/(D_word-3a^2/2), by the same proof whenever its denominator is
positive. No estimate of z_0 is assumed here.

On (2), D_word=D_m and S>n=2^m-1>=m>=1536, so D_m>3073>760.
The accepted comparison in fact931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/D_m<47/50.
Applying it in its correct direction
to the positive UPPER bound (29),

    B_n E < (47/50) D_m/(D_m-8) < 19/20.                    (31)

The last inequality is equivalent, with positive denominators, to D_m>760.
Also D_m>136 yields

    E<=1/(D_m-8)<17/(16D_m).                                 (32)

Equation (32) holds only for THIS constructed class. It does not meet the
full-function hypothesis of the conditional theorem in the accepted fact.
It cannot be substituted for W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m).

Because the COMPLETE K_n(H_m)>=1/D_m and F_ray_n(H_m)>=K_n(H_m), we also have

    E/K_n(H_m)<=D_m/(D_m-8),
    E/F_ray_n(H_m)<=D_m/(D_m-8).                             (33)

Both comparison denominators retain every original leaf, bridge, and
feasible checkpoint choice. Neither is replaced by a selected local cut.
Since D_m>1+2(2^m-1) tends to infinity, the right sides tend to one uniformly
over the whole law. Combining (29) and (30) further gives

    1 <= D_m sup_(this law) E <= D_m/(D_m-8) -> 1.           (34)

Thus the new face rotation has been made legal and quantitatively priced,
but it cannot deliver a nonvanishing benchmark gain in this count and
terminal domain. The actual optimum inside the narrow bracket is not
evaluated. In particular (33) does not assert E<=K_n or E<=F_ray_n.

## 8. Scope attacks and the remaining vector question

The direct-proving screen has established, inside this one unverified draft,
the legal real geometry, all guards, own radius, noncollinearity, exact
terminal formula, and the uniform comparison for the declared law.

The counterexample screen found two reasons not to overextend it:

1. The metric bound kappa<=12 genuinely uses q>=r. For the algebraically
   same face geometry with q=1 and arbitrary integer r, the exact metric
   ratio is kappa=3(3r+1), which is unbounded. For a terminal triangle
   vertex, eta=(2/3)kappa=2(3r+1), also unbounded. This refutes extending
   the particular estimate eta<=8 to all copied three-group counts. It
   does NOT refute a performance bound there, because the initial
   transverse charge kappa||z_0||^2 remains to be paid. No second law is
   claimed analyzed on that larger domain.
2. The terminal restriction z_n in K is also load-bearing. For a general
   transverse terminal z, formula (9) has 2Phi(z), not ||z||^2, and
   2Phi(z) is unbounded along an outward vertex ray: for
   w=e_1-(1,1,1)/3 and t>=1, the normal condition gives P_K(tw)=w,
   so 2Phi(tw)=(2t-1)||w||^2=(2t-1)*2/3. The inequality
   2Phi(z)<=2/3 cannot simply be imported outside K. This is a scope
   failure, not a realized high-error trajectory on H_m.

These checks invalidate the two tempting extensions before either is used
as a proof step. They leave a precise open direction: the actual
two-dimensional triangle profile with no uniformly positive inactive
fraction, or with unrestricted terminal amplitudes, needs a joint estimate
of terminal value versus the complete initial transverse norm. The present
bounded-terminal carrier proof supplies no such estimate. It also excludes
neither a full simplex with no permanently inactive coordinates nor a rule
whose participating group union changes over the whole history.

No fixed two-group tuning, independent Cartesian inverse duplication,
coordinate creation/splitting, free carrier, expected face, unpriced extreme
coordinate, or function change was used. The scalar-amplitude lane remains
separate: the transverse process here has three noncollinear gradients.

## 9. Retrieval, resource record, and handoff status

Skills applied: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and
construct-counterexamples. The active search was a mutation of the mature
centered-simplex inverse program, targeting all-history support rotation
with a fully paid radius.

Matlas returned 1808.02681 Theorem4.1, 2012.00990 Proposition4, and
1410.7012 Lemma36; none supplied the required GD mechanism or entered this
proof. Web search located the standard simplex threshold reference:
Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan, "Projection onto the probability
simplex: An efficient algorithm with a simple proof, and an application",
arXiv:1309.1541 (2013), https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.1541. The exact local
extracted paper downloads/1309.1541_public_pdf_text.txt, Section3, including
Theorem1 and its KKT proof, was read. It concerns Euclidean projection of
a finite real list onto nonnegative weights summing to one. Its threshold
normal-condition method informs (7)-(12); all required claims are proved
here directly. No external mathematical result is an unproved premise.

Some initial broad batched retrieval outputs were truncated; the selected
inputs and scopes were subsequently read in bounded calls. No conclusion
is based on unseen truncated text. The accepted benchmark fact was read
in full through its final conditional conclusion.

Resource use: one analytical invocation; one substantive mechanism;
ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic trajectory replays,
executed recurrences, optimizers, solvers, benchmark DP, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, paid consults, or subagents. File writing,
text retrieval, text parsing, and integrity/readback checks only.

This report is to be published as a full global-memory proof_attempt with

# Exact value-flow prices and two complete redundancy results

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. This is max2's first analytical round
after completed baseline22, under TASK.md and guidance f7608aa358751108.
No numerical evaluation, solver, parameter scan, replay, machine algebra,
formal verification, installation, or subordinate agent was used.

Accepted inputs: 04219554e41d9750, Section10 (the ORIGINAL primitive
certificate), and 1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraphs1--2 (all-pair smooth
convex interpolation and its actual conjugate construction). Everything
else needed below is proved in this document. Earlier C82/C83 and coupling
drafts are comparison targets, never accepted premises.

The required concrete outcome is exact redundancy of value pricing on
the ENTIRE unit-curvature repaired-cut family, including all its legal
repair amplitudes, arbitrary nonnegative words, zero steps, and all
original coordinates. The lifted-cut geometry in2948d4cd3660413f also has
identically zero price. A separate C82 calculation gives an exact price
and a fully minimized formula in a specified parameter domain. It proves
redundancy there when a^2+a<=1; its complementary regime is not asserted
to resolve a surviving full-cone region.

The original asymptotic target, existence/nonexistence of a threshold
seed, and general four-step cone reversal remain OPEN. No new fact is
asserted and no theorem below is promoted past the verifier gate.

## 1. Original orientation, objective, and formal coordinates

Let n>=0, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative word, s=sum h_i,
C=1+s, and I={0,...,n}. Keep every original gradient coordinate, including
the terminal coordinate and every coordinate at a zero step. Fixed
physical vectors x_i,g_i in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space obey

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

For sampled values f_i put

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.       (1)

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proves Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair of actual
smooth convex samples, and constructs a single interpolant from these
inequalities. Assume initially that all these finite inequalities hold.
An optimum sample will be retained explicitly when it is part of the
fixed geometry; Section3 also handles the case where its location is free.

For the original nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij, put Lambda_ii=0 and

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
    L^T e=omega.                                         (2)

Here e is the (n+1)-entry all-ones column. Thus omega_i is outgoing minus
incoming flow. The TERMINAL is the source, of mass2s, and index i<n is
a sink of mass2h_i. Reversing these arrows gives the wrong value problem.

With T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, let

    D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n, -s(s+2)),
    M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives, on every physical realization,

    V(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.   (3)

The full certificate requires M PSD on ALL of R^(n+1). Nothing below
deletes a coordinate, restricts Lambda support in that feasibility
question, or identifies formal gradients at zero steps. Sparse flows
constructed below solve an auxiliary transport minimum; they are NOT
claimed to have PSD matrices.

## 2. An exact finite price, with elementary proof and attained flow

Fix legal base values f_i and put w_ij=-Q_ij>=0 for distinct i,j. Regard
w_ij as the cost of the directed arc i->j. Let d_i be the least cost of
a path from n to i, with d_n=0. The complete finite graph makes every
distance finite. A minimizing path exists: a repeated-vertex segment
has nonnegative cost and can be deleted, so it is enough to consider
finitely many simple paths.

For replacement values f_i+z_i, the complete constraints are exactly

    z_i-z_j<=w_ij.                                      (4)

Summing along an n->i path gives z_n-z_i<=its cost. Therefore

    z_i-z_n>=-d_i,
    V(f+z)>=V(f)-Pflow,
    Pflow=2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.                             (5)

All h_i are nonnegative, including possibly zero. The simultaneous
choice z_i=-d_i attains every lower bound in(5): the distance inequality
d_j<=d_i+w_ij is precisely its feasibility in(4). Thus this is an
attained minimum of the value problem, not only a lower bound from a
dual program.

The same number is the exact minimum slack cost of the ORIGINAL flow:

    min_{Lambda>=0, L^T e=omega} sum Lambda_ij w_ij
       =Pflow.                                         (6)

To prove the lower bound, multiply w_ij>=d_j-d_i by Lambda_ij and sum.
The result is

    sum Lambda_ij w_ij >= -sum_i omega_i d_i
                         =2sum_(i<n)h_i d_i.

For attainment, choose a shortest n->i path for every i<n with h_i>0
and send mass2h_i along that path. Sum these path flows. Intermediate
vertices have equal incoming and outgoing mass; the endpoints give
exactly(2). Each path contributes2h_i d_i. Zero-weight sinks need no
path, but their vertices and all their available arcs remain present.

Combining(3) with(6) gives a necessary condition for any full certificate:

    V(f)>=Pflow.                                        (7)

The selected optimal values give the same condition as V(f-d)>=0. Indeed
their new slacks are w'_ij=w_ij+d_i-d_j, and every prescribed-flow array
satisfies

    sum Lambda_ij w'_ij=sum Lambda_ij w_ij-Pflow.

The Gram matrix and the formal certificate matrix do not change with
the values. The full PSD restriction can impose more than the minimum
over flows in(6); it has not been dropped from the original problem.

### Zero costs, coincident gradients, and fixed optimum constraints

No strict positivity of costs is needed. A zero-cost directed cycle
has every arc of cost zero, and(4) then forces equal shifts around that
cycle. Distances and the path-flow construction remain valid without
choosing a unique shortest path.

If two base gradients coincide, Q_ij+Q_ji=0, so legality forces BOTH
residuals to vanish. Hence replacement shifts at these two samples are
equal, even when their positions differ. If two base positions coincide,
the sum of the two residuals is ||g_i-g_j||^2; legality first forces
coincident gradients and then coincident values. These are properties of
actual jets, not extra formal equations in the PSD certificate.

One may also include a fixed optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0 as an
extra graph vertex. Let d_i^* be distances in this larger graph. Then
z_i=d_star^*-d_i^* is feasible, has z_star=0, and minimizes the same
value objective subject to ALL finite and optimum pairs. Its improvement
is2sum h_i d_i^*. In general d_i^*<=d_i, so this price need not equal
the original finite-flow price. They WILL agree in every concrete family
below. The distinction is important: optimum edges are not original
primitive multipliers and cannot silently be used as such.

## 3. The optimized finite values give one static function and real minima

This section also explains why(5), without a fixed optimum vertex, is
not merely a formal jet result. For any legal replacement values f'_i
on the finite samples, set

    y_i=x_i-g_i, beta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f'_i,
    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I){<y_i,v>+beta_i},
    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (8)

This is the construction in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, written out here
to identify its minimizers. H is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex,
and has quadratic growth. The supremum has one maximizer v(x). Strong
subgradient inequalities give

    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2,

and the maximizing inequalities give

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>
      <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Thus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with
gradient v(x). The all-pair inequalities say that at v=g_i the i-th
quadratic in H is active and has gradient x_i. Consequently v(x_i)=g_i
and F(x_i)=f'_i. This is ONE fixed F used for all steps.

Let beta_max=max_i beta_i, and A={i:beta_i=beta_max}. The exact minimizer
set of F is

    argmin F=conv{y_i:i in A},   min F=-beta_max.          (9)

Indeed gradient F(x)=0 is equivalent to x being a subgradient of H at0.
The subgradients there are exactly the convex hull of the active affine
slopes y_i. To check the reverse inclusion without an unproved formula,
if x is outside that convex hull, a separating vector u has
<x,u> greater than every <y_i,u> for i in A. For sufficiently small
positive t, inactive affine pieces remain inactive at tu, and
H(tu)-H(0)=t max_(i in A)<y_i,u>+t^2||u||^2/2,
contradicting the subgradient inequality for x. The forward inclusion
follows by convexly combining the active supporting inequalities.

In particular a real minimizer always exists, even if none was fixed in
the graph. The true initial distance is exactly

    dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A}).                           (10)

No arbitrary radius or changed function is inserted into the GD run.
The specific families below either preserve the optimum0 or identify
the closest minimizer explicitly.

## 4. Entire unit-curvature repaired-cut family: price exactly zero

This is the main complete redundancy result. It works for any n>=1,
any nonnegative word, and any cut j with0<=j<n and h_j>1. Put

    P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t,
    eta=(h_j-1)/(1+T_j)>0,
    p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) for0<=i<=j.

Choose an amplitude epsilon>=0. Define two scalar coordinate arrays:

    xB_i=1+sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, gB_i=1        (i<=j),
    xA_i=gA_i=p_i                            (i<=j),

    R_k=sum_(t=k)^(n-1)h_t                    (k>j),
    xB_k=-eta*(1+R_k), gB_k=-eta              (k>j),
    xA_k=p_j*xB_k, gA_k=-eta*p_j              (k>j).

Empty sums have value zero. At the cut,
xB_(j+1)=1-h_j=-eta*(1+T_j), and the same equality multiplied by p_j
holds for coordinate A. Before the cut the A coordinate follows unit
quadratic GD. After it both coordinates have constant gradients. Hence
the vectors

    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),
    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2                            (11)

obey every original GD equation. Include X_star=G_star=F_star=0.

Here are ALL interpolation pairs. For saturated values(11),

    Q_ik=<G_i-G_k,X_k-G_k>.

For a prefix target k<=j, its normal is

    X_k-G_k=(sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t,0).

Every prefix source has residual zero. Every tail source has residual
-(1+eta)sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0. The optimum source has residual
-sum_(t=k)^(j-1)h_t<=0.

For a tail target k>j, its normal is

    X_k-G_k=-eta R_k*(1,sqrt(epsilon)*p_j).

Every tail source has residual zero. A prefix source i<=j has residual

    -eta R_k*[1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)].           (12)

The optimum source has residual
-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2)<=0. Every pair into the optimum is zero.
These cases exhaust all directed finite and optimum pairs.

If T_j>0, legality is EXACTLY the finite set of inequalities

    1+eta+epsilon*p_j*(p_i+eta*p_j)>=0   for i<=j.        (13)

The necessity follows by using target j+1, where R_(j+1)=T_j>0.
If T_j=0, every tail normal vanishes and no condition(13) is necessary.
Zero p_j, zero epsilon, zero intermediate steps and repeated gradients
cause no exception in these formulas. This specifies the full legal
repair-amplitude family, rather than only its most-used endpoint.

For EVERY legal epsilon, the finite zero-cost arcs include

    n->j,
    j->i for every i<j,
    n->k for every j<k<n,
    i->n for every i<n.                                 (14)

The first and last groups hold because X_j=G_j and X_n=G_n. The second
group holds because both sources and targets are prefix samples. The
third holds because all tail gradients coincide. Thus every original
sample is in the zero-cost component of n, so

    d_i=d_i^*=0 for EVERY i,
    Pflow=0.                                           (15)

Equivalently, all feasible value replacements have z_i=z_n. With the
fixed zero optimum, Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=0 also forces z_n=0. Thus ALL
sampled values in(11) are forced, not merely optimal for this objective.

An explicit attaining ORIGINAL flow sends2sum_(i<=j)h_i from n to j,
sends2h_i from j to i for i<j, and sends2h_k directly from n to k for
j<k<n. Its other entries vanish. The balance is(2), and every used arc
is in(14). It has zero cost. This does not assert its matrix is PSD.

There is one actual static smooth convex witness for every legal
amplitude: use(8) with the zero optimum included. For its prefix samples,
beta_i=-sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t; for its tail samples,
beta_k=-eta^2 R_k*(1+epsilon*p_j^2). These constants are nonpositive,
and equality in either formula implies that the corresponding normal
is zero. The optimum sample also has constant and normal zero. Thus(9)
gives the UNIQUE minimizer0 for this single conjugate interpolant. Its
TRUE squared initial distance is(1+P_j)^2+epsilon, in every case above.

The projection construction used in the earlier drafts is also valid:
let K=conv({0} union {G_i}) and define

    F(X)=max_(G in K){<X,G>-||G||^2/2}.                  (16)

The complete normal list proves that the optimizer at X_i is G_i.
Projection onto K is1-Lipschitz: add the two projection normal
inequalities to obtain ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. The same maximizing
inequalities used in Section3 prove differentiability. Since0 is in K,
F>=0=F(0). This is a real minimizer, and(11) are its actual values.

The saturated-value telescoping identity is

    V=||X_0||^2-||X_n+sG_n||^2.

To check it, use2h_i F_i+h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2
=||X_i||^2-||X_(i+1)||^2, sum, and substitute F_n.
Here X_n=G_n, so the family gives exactly

    V=(1+P_j)^2+epsilon
         -C^2 eta^2*(1+epsilon*p_j^2).                  (17)

Reoptimizing values adds NO inequality to nonnegativity of(17), for
any legal epsilon, word, cut or zero-step pattern. Consequently it adds
none to an existing collection of necessary conditions that already
includes this ENTIRE family. This is a family-level exact redundancy
claim, not global redundancy for arbitrary queried geometries.

### The actual four-step C83 member and true radius

Take h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), a,b,c,d>0, j=2, and

    C=5+a+b+c+d, Q=3+a+b, eta=c/(2+d), p_2=ab.

The only possibly negative coefficient in(13) is i=1, where p_1=-a.
If b eta<1, the full amplitude interval is

    0<=epsilon<=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta)).                (18)

The other two coefficients have positive p_i. The endpoint in(18)
is exactly the repaired-cut value used in d3abee1b17da52da; there is
no new function-value freedom at ANY amplitude in this interval.

For0<ab<1 and epsilon>0, the two prefix gradients
(1,sqrt(epsilon)) and(1,-a sqrt(epsilon)) have G_2 in the relative
interior of their segment. Since G_3=-eta G_2, zero is in the interior
of their nondegenerate triangle with G_3. Thus the minimizer of(16) is
uniquely0: a nonzero normal at0 would have positive pairing with a
point of an open ball in K. The TRUE squared distance is Q^2+epsilon.
At epsilon=0 the instance is scalar and0 is again the closest minimizer
to the displayed initial point. No benchmark ratio is inferred from V.

## 5. The lifted-cut coupling geometry also has zero value price

This section reconstructs the actual geometry used in draft
2948d4cd3660413f, independently of that draft's quantitative matrix gap.
Let a,b,c,d>0, put u=1+a, C=5+a+b+c+d, H=3+c+d, eta=b/H,
and assume a eta<1. Define

    nu=(1+eta)(1-a eta)/a>0,
    U=(1,0), Z=-eta U+sqrt(nu)*(0,1),
    X=(U+uZ, U, -bU, -(2+d)eta U, -eta U),
    G=(Z, U, -eta U, -eta U, -eta U),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (19)

The original four updates follow from b=H eta. Include the zero optimum.
The normals at targets1 and4 vanish. At target0 the normal is U+aZ;
the source1 residual is zero, and each source2,3,4 residual is -a nu.
The optimum-source residual there is -(1-a eta)<0. At target2 the
normal is -(b-eta)U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual
-(1+eta)(b-eta), and tail sources have residual zero. At target3 the
normal is -(1+d)eta U: source0 has residual zero, source1 has residual
-(1+eta)(1+d)eta, and tail sources have residual zero. Optimum-source
residuals at2,3 are respectively -eta(b-eta) and-(1+d)eta^2.
Every residual into the optimum is zero. This checks every pair.

In particular the zero-cost finite paths are

    4->1->0, 4->2, 4->3, and i->4 for every i<4.        (20)

All value differences are therefore forced, and the optimum pairs fix
their common shift. Both finite-flow and fixed-optimum prices vanish.
The function(16) with K=conv{0,Z,U,-eta U} realizes these samples.
Its minimizer set is{(0,t):t<=0}; the initial second coordinate is
u sqrt(nu)>0, so0 is the closest minimizer. Its true squared initial
distance is ||U+uZ||^2, not an independent calibration variable.

The energy is exactly

    Erow=||U+uZ||^2-C^2 eta^2
        =a+3+1/a-[(1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a]eta-C^2 eta^2.     (21)

Value-flow pricing returns only Erow>=0. It cannot recover a strictly
positive lower bound on Erow on this SAME geometry: the auxiliary flow
minimum is attained at zero and the values have no remaining freedom.
The further full-matrix restrictions in2948d4cd3660413f use PSD coupling
with a different quadratic direction and the first row. This result
neither assumes that unverified coupling theorem nor refutes it; it
identifies exactly what the present price can and cannot add to it.

## 6. C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant

Here keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let

    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,
    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},
    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).

Use parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate
triangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.
These inequalities imply t>=0. Define

    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),
    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),
    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),
    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),
    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)

Every GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,
K=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).
Its comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,

    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,

all nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal
is(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1
gives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1
residual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient
sources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is
(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2
for source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite
and optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.

The finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.
The least cost into0 is

    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)

The other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no
smaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while
each minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore

    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,
    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)

The complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by

    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)

All incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive
by(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are
unchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including
the optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.

For the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.
Its affine constants beta_i are

    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,
    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,
    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.

When t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its
minimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the
closest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.
If t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same
closest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is

    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.

The base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are

    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,
    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)

In particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are
always rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price
whenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed
actual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.

## 7. Full C82 minimum for0<a<1,0<b<=sqrt(3),ab<=1

Assume0<a<1,0<b<=sqrt(3),ab<=1, and keep c,d>0 arbitrary and ell as
above. This domain includes the compact-domain inequalities used in the
earlier threshold drafts, when those inequalities are taken as explicit
hypotheses; no unverified global reduction is being assumed here. Put

    R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a),
    v_cross=[1+ell/(b+1)]/a,
    v_stationary=a/(1-a),
    v_best=min{v_cross,max{R,v_stationary}},
    K=C^2 eta^2.                                       (27)

Then R>=1/a>=b and R<=v_cross. The first assertion follows because
Htri has its nonnegative zero at1/a and is increasing thereafter. For
the second, Htri(R)=ell implies(b+1)(aR-1)<=ell.

The exact infimum of(26) over EVERY legal triangle parameter is

    Enew=(1-a^2)v_best^2-2(1+a)a v_best+1+2(1+a)
                +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.                 (28)

If ell>0 the minimizing triangle has z=sqrt(ell)>0 and v=v_best.
If ell=0 the same formula is attained by the displayed degenerate
legal data; alternatively it is the limit of nondegenerate triangles.

Here is the complete minimization. For fixed v>=R, the price switches
at z^2=(b+1)t, which is no larger than Htri(v) because v>=b. Below
this value its coefficient of z^2 is1-b^2; above it the coefficient
is1-b^2+2(1+a)>=2a>0. If b<=1, z^2=ell is a minimizer. If b>1, the
minimizer is z^2=max{ell,(b+1)t}. Thus for R<=v<=v_cross BOTH cases
use z^2=ell, the second term in(23) is the minimum, and(26) becomes

    J_left(v)=(1-a^2)v^2-2(1+a)a v+1+2(1+a)
                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.         (29)

For v>=v_cross and b<=1 it becomes

    J_right(v)=(1-a^2)v^2+2(1+a)ab v+1-2(1+a)b
                              +(1-b^2)ell-K.           (30)

J_right has strictly positive derivative on that half-line. If b>1,
the corresponding expression for v>=v_cross is instead

    J_kink(v)=(1-a^2)v^2
        +a[2(1+a)b+(1-b^2)(b+1)]v
        +1-2(1+a)b-(1-b^2)(b+1)-K.                    (30a)

Its derivative is positive for v>=1/a. To see this, divide that
derivative by a and use v>=1/a and ab<=1:

    J_kink'(v)/a
      >=2/a^2-2+2(1+a)b-(b^2-1)(b+1)
      >=2(1+a)b-(b^2-1)(b-1)
      >=2(1+a)b-2(b-1)=2(ab+1)>0.

The last inequality uses b^2<=3 and b>1. Each right expression agrees
with J_left at v_cross. Hence in either b regime no minimum occurs
strictly after v_cross. J_left is a strictly convex quadratic with
stationary point a/(1-a). Clipping that point to[R,v_cross] proves
(27)--(28), including both endpoint cases.

The original unpriced C82 minimum in this domain is

    Eold=((1+a)R-1)^2+(1-b^2)ell-K,                    (31)

For b<=1 its energy increases in v and is minimized at z^2=ell. For
b>1 its minimizing z^2 at fixed v is Htri(v). The resulting quadratic
has leading coefficient(1+a)^2+(1-b^2)a>=1+a^2>0 and derivative at
v=1/a equal to(1+a)[2/a+1-b^2]>0, since a<1 and b^2<=3. Its derivative
therefore stays positive for v>=R>=1/a, and its minimum is at R too.
At v=R, Htri(R)=ell makes Dprice=0, and J_left(R)=Eold.

Consequently value pricing adds EXACTLY NO information to this fully
optimized C82 condition whenever

    R>=a/(1-a).                                        (32)

In particular(32) holds throughout a^2+a<=1, because R>=1/a.
The positive root of a^2+a=1 is(sqrt(5)-1)/2. This includes the
first-step projection range39/100<=a<=41/100 from d3abee1b17da52da,
with its b range79/100<=b<=81/100. The C83 price was already zero there
by Section4, independently of this comparison.

When ell>0 and R<a/(1-a), one has v_cross>R and v_best>R, so the
optimized energy is strictly smaller. The precise positive reduction is

    Eold-Enew=(1-a^2)*[(R-v_stationary)^2
                                 -(v_best-v_stationary)^2]>0. (33)

Equivalently this strict-improvement regime is

    a^2+a>1,
    0<ell<(a^2+a-1)/(1-a)^2.                            (34)

This proves a strict improvement of the C82 FAMILY VALUE in its stated
domain. It does NOT prove that a word satisfying all the other current
necessary conditions is rejected by(28). In particular(34) alone is
not a surviving full-cone region and must not be advertised as one.

## 8. Comparison, resources, and the precise remaining interface

* C83: Section4 completely resolves the proposed value-price mechanism
  for all legal amplitudes of all unit-curvature repaired cuts. The
  optimum/source flow has an explicit zero-cost realization; the full
  value system is rigid. This includes the actual unit-curvature j=2
  separator in d3abee1b17da52da. Curvatures strictly between0 and1 are
  NOT covered by this rigidity theorem.

* C82: Sections6--7 solve the fixed geometry, construct its optimized
  actual function, and eliminate both geometric parameters in the
  explicitly stated domain. A positive price at a nonoptimal triangle
  cannot be called a new word obstruction when(32) holds.

* 554ec17f7f590bf0 and d3abee1b17da52da: their endpoint core is not
  being declared empty. These findings contain further triangle,
  clipping and endpoint conditions; no claim of their complete
  redundancy is made. On the specified projected prism, the tested
  C82/C83 value re-selections add no new condition beyond those
  already present.

* 2948d4cd3660413f: Section5 gives an exact zero-price result for its
  actual lifted-cut geometry, with every pair reconstructed. A
  transport minimum cannot replace that draft's simultaneous full
  PSD/first-row analysis. Its quantitative gap remains a separate
  unverified joint result.

* 04219554e41d9750: its ORIGINAL twenty-sign, exact-flow, full R5 PSD
  interface remains the relevant sufficient seed condition. Every
  implication here uses precisely that identity. No sparse flow
  constructed for pricing is promoted to a feasible certificate.

Two concrete analytical directions remain for the next authorized
round: decide whether the explicit C82 improvement(28) excludes a
region surviving the OTHER full necessary conditions; or apply the
exact price to a genuinely non-unit-curvature geometry, where zero
paths need not pin the values. Either needs an actual static function
and a comparison on the same word. Neither has been decided here.

Retrieval: Matlas returned unrelated results to the complete-statement
value/transport query. The web fallback recovered the already locally
available Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur1502.05666. Its Theorem4 proof,
Corollary1, and cycle Remark3 were read, as were elementary shortest-path
duality notes. No external theorem is a proof input: the accepted
interpolation fact and the direct path argument above suffice.

The complete draft is one joint argument; its sections are not accepted
building blocks. No fact_submit call is authorized or was made. This
round does not use or renew any numerical allocation. It does not close
the original project or select a final answer.
